**Background**: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) are a technique for interpreting language model representations by decomposing polysemantic activations into sparse, interpretable features. TopK SAEs are a variant that directly controls the number of active features using a TopK activation function. A key challenge is distinguishing stable features from artifacts of random initialization, typically addressed by training multiple seeds and comparing features via cosine similarity, which is computationally expensive.

## [REI Learns Ideation Policies from Repository Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33183) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose REI, a Repository Evolution-driven Ideation framework that learns reusable ideation policies from code repository evolution, injecting domain-specific technical experience into model parameters. They also identify semantic diversity collapse and introduce Semantic Prior Optimization and Debiasing (SPOD) to preserve method diversity. Ideation is becoming a bottleneck in automated scientific and engineering iteration, and REI offers a scalable way to learn from repository history instead of costly trial-and-error. The identification of semantic diversity collapse highlights a hidden failure mode in quality-driven ideation optimization that could limit cross-task generalization. REI treats code changes as concrete technical hypotheses and uses subsequent revisions, adoption, and reuse as utility signals. SPOD makes method identity explicit, corrects category-level reward bias, and limits redundant accumulation within the same method family while retaining strong representatives.

**Background**: Automated scientific discovery systems increasingly rely on large language models to generate and test ideas, but useful ideas are sparse and validation is expensive. ## [Lean 4 mechanization of open-source game theory resolves DupocBot/CupodBot problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33193) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper provides formal foundations for open-source game theory by specifying a concrete proof system, resolving an open problem about DupocBot and CupodBot via a transposition argument, and mechanizing the framework in Lean 4. It also extends the framework to partial transparency and studies cooperation in Prisoner's Dilemma experiments. This work is significant for AI alignment because it shows that controlling how much agents can know about one another affects the sustainability of cooperation, which models collusion. It provides a formal, mechanized foundation for reasoning about multi-agent collusion risks. The mechanization in Lean 4 is claimed to be the first for resource-bounded open-source game theory. In Prisoner's Dilemma experiments, greater transparency increases both the cooperation sustainable in equilibrium and the cooperation selected by replicator dynamics.

## [CogLoadBench isolates extraneous cognitive load in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33197) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce CogLoadBench, a controlled benchmark that varies five presentation factors inspired by Cognitive Load Theory across three reasoning task families, and propose the Extraneous Load Score (ELS), an activation-based metric that estimates a model's extraneous load at prompt time. They also show that shifting model representations toward lower-load directions at inference can improve average performance without changing the prompt. This work systematically separates presentation effects from task difficulty, offering a new way to diagnose and mitigate performance drops caused by prompt formatting, which could inform better prompt engineering and more robust model evaluation. It also demonstrates that internal load representations can be steered to improve reasoning outcomes. ELS is computed from model activations and generalizes across reasoning task types and load levels, and the mitigation works by shifting representations at inference time without altering prompt content. The benchmark covers five CLT-inspired presentation factors and three reasoning task families, though the abstract does not specify model sizes or the magnitude of gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Cognitive Load Theory distinguishes intrinsic load (from task difficulty) from extraneous load (from how information is presented), and both affect human problem-solving. Large language models show analogous sensitivity to presentation, but prior work has not cleanly isolated extraneous load from task difficulty. CogLoadBench fills this gap by manipulating presentation factors while holding task content constant, and ELS provides a quantitative handle on the resulting internal load.

An analysis of U.S. SEC filings from 2017 to 2025 found that AI-related passage intensity increased 6.3-fold between 2017-2022 and 2023-2025, while the operational share of AI disclosure fell from 25.76% to 13.45%. Approximately 89% of the net increase in AI disclosure intensity fell outside the operational category, indicating dilution by nonoperational corporate AI narratives. The findings are relevant for researchers, regulators, and investors who rely on SEC filings to assess corporate AI adoption. The study combines frozen language-model predictions for 26,279 AI-related passages with a human audit of 1,000 annotated passages, and the decline in operational share persists within sectors, filing types, and in 10-K-only analysis. The authors emphasize that more operational disclosure can coexist with a lower operational share, meaning operational disclosure was diluted rather than displaced.

## [How Many Samples Is RL Worth? Best-of-N versus RL Post-Training for Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33232) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper measures the 'exchange rate' between best-of-N sampling from a base text-to-image model and one sample from an RL post-trained model, using Stable Diffusion 3.5 Medium and its Flow-GRPO checkpoint on 398 GenEval-style prompts with human labels and two automatic judges. It finds that on prompts without positional constraints, best-of-16 with a VLM picker is roughly equivalent to one RL image, but on positional prompts RL is far ahead, and that automatic judges disagree in opposite directions. This work provides practitioners with actionable guidance on when to invest in expensive RL post-training versus simply drawing more samples and using a picker, and it highlights that automatic evaluation metrics can mislead comparisons between RL and best-of-N. It affects teams deploying text-to-image models who need to decide on compute allocation and evaluation methodology. The study uses 398 new prompts in the style of GenEval, with human labels on every prompt and two automatic judges other than the reward; the primary test requires both automatic judges to pass an image and finds best-of-16 with a VLM picker 9.3 points behind one RL image, while human checks show a smaller gap of 5.5 and 6.5 points. RL also solves 20 prompts that the base model misses in 128 tries, and 1,024 tries recover half of them, and FLUX.1-dev shows a similar gap.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) with automatically checkable rewards, such as GenEval's object and position checks, has become a common final training step for text-to-image models like Stable Diffusion 3.5. Best-of-N is a simpler alternative: generate N images from the base model and use a picker, often a vision-language model (VLM), to select the best one. This paper quantifies how many base samples are equivalent to one RL sample, a ratio they call the exchange rate.

## [Diffusion autoencoder generates privacy-aware synthetic chest CT with diagnostic utility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33234) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers developed a diffusion autoencoder framework that generates class-conditional synthetic chest CT images by sampling latent representations from class-specific kernel density estimates and filtering out samples too close to training data. Models trained solely on synthetic data achieved an AUC of 0.862 for pulmonary embolism classification, close to the 0.872 AUC of models trained on real data. This work addresses the critical need for privacy-preserving medical data sharing, showing that synthetic images can retain diagnostic information for downstream tasks while reducing the risk of reproducing patient data. It could enable broader collaboration and AI development in healthcare without compromising patient privacy. The framework was trained on the RSNA 2020 Pulmonary Embolism dataset, and a nearest-neighbor copy-detection analysis of 38,000 synthetic slices found no direct copies of real training images. ## [Distribution-Free Sequential Testing via Unsupervised Model Errors for Data Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33238) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes two distribution-free test statistics for binary Sequential Hypothesis Testing (SHT) and Sequential Change Point Detection (SCPD) that monitor the errors of a predictive or reconstructive machine learning model trained solely on null/pre-change data. It establishes strict non-asymptotic error control for SHT and explicit bounds on Average Run Length (ARL) and Expected Detection Delay (EDD) for SCPD under mild assumptions. This framework addresses practical challenges in streaming environments where data are too complex for conventional likelihood ratio tests and where only null data are available, such as zero-day attacks or emerging diseases. It enables scalable, distribution-free detection with analytical guarantees, potentially improving anomaly detection in network security and other high-dimensional applications. The methods operate on scalar errors without requiring online retraining or kernel evaluations, making them highly scalable. Evaluations in target tracking, chaotic dynamical systems, and real-world network security datasets show the framework consistently satisfies prescribed constraints, whereas existing algorithms suffer from inflated missed detections and/or false alarms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Sequential Hypothesis Testing (SHT) is a statistical method for deciding between two hypotheses as data arrive, controlling error rates. Sequential Change Point Detection (SCPD) identifies the time when the distribution of a data stream changes. Traditional likelihood ratio-based tests require exact probabilistic modeling, which is often infeasible for complex high-dimensional data. Distribution-free tests avoid assuming a specific distribution, and unsupervised models trained on normal data can detect anomalies via imperfect generalization.

## [CypherMAS: Benchmarking Routing Reliability in LLM Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33242) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CypherMAS is a synthetic benchmark that isolates communication and routing in fixed multi-agent graphs (chain, star, bipartite, ring) by having agents route ciphered messages to peers with matching decoders. It measures routing failures and redundant LLM calls against a known optimal path across eleven open-weight models from 4B to 753B parameters. This benchmark addresses a gap in existing evaluations that couple communication with open-domain tasks, making it hard to attribute failures to reasoning, tool use, or message loss. By identifying communication reliability as a distinct scaling bottleneck, it provides a controlled diagnostic tool that could influence how multi-agent systems are designed and evaluated. The benchmark reveals that small models fail at routing and tool use, dense models above 27B degrade with graph diameter, and even successful runs exceed the minimum number of LLM calls. The source code is freely available on GitHub, enabling reproducibility and further research.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Multi-agent systems (MAS) built on large language models distribute reasoning across specialized agents that must exchange messages and invoke tools reliably. Existing benchmarks often mix communication with open-domain tasks, so it is difficult to tell whether a failure comes from reasoning, malformed tool use, lost messages, or forgotten requests. CypherMAS isolates routing by using ciphered messages and fixed graph topologies where the optimal path is known, allowing precise measurement of routing failures and redundancy.

## [FGSM Outperforms PGD in Transferable Adversarial Malware Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33243) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces the Adversarial AI Engine (AAIE) for automated security stress-testing and reports that single-step FGSM achieves a 52.9% attack success rate against a Random Forest malware detector, compared to 45.2% for iterative PGD (p < 0.002). This counterintuitive finding challenges the common assumption that iterative attacks like PGD are always stronger, suggesting that simpler attacks can produce more transferable adversarial examples and that robustness evaluations must account for transferability. It has direct implications for ML security practices and AI governance standards. The study used the EMBER2018 dataset and benchmarked attacks with ASR, transferability score, evasion rate, and resilience index, finding transferability scores peak at ~0.8 under constrained perturbations. The authors attribute PGD's lower transferability to overfitting surrogate networks, which creates brittle perturbations.

Transferability refers to whether adversarial examples crafted on one model also fool another, which is crucial for black-box attacks where the target model is unknown. EMBER2018 is a widely used open dataset of static features from Portable Executable (PE) malware files for training and evaluating malware classifiers.

## [First Measurement Study Links Anti-AI Toxic Discourse to Real-World Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33254) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint presents the first measurement study of online conflict over generative AI, analyzing 931,640 Reddit posts from 25 communities to quantify toxic discourse and identify 3,312 anti-AI actions such as crawler tarpits and data-poisoning services. The study finds that 30.0% of posts in conflict-focused communities are toxic, compared to 4.4% in general AI communities, and traces the output of three deployed anti-AI tools. This study provides the first large-scale empirical evidence linking toxic online discourse to concrete anti-AI actions that can contaminate public datasets like Common Crawl and FineWeb, raising urgent questions for content moderation and AI training data integrity. It highlights a growing sociotechnical phenomenon that could affect the quality and trustworthiness of future generative AI models. The analysis reveals that 66.5% of code samples from a data-poisoning service contain fabricated facts, misused APIs, or broken logic, yet 93.6% passes a public code-curation pipeline, close to the 95.0% rate for real source code. Additionally, adding a period to each paragraph of crawler tarpit output increases its curation survival rate from 16.4% to 91.9%, and both tarpit and poisoning outputs have already entered Common Crawl and FineWeb.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Generative AI models are trained on massive datasets scraped from the web, such as Common Crawl and FineWeb. In response, some communities have deployed anti-AI tools like crawler tarpits, which trap web crawlers in endless loops, and data-poisoning services, which inject corrupted data to degrade model performance. This study is the first to systematically measure the prevalence of toxic discourse and the deployment of such tools, and to assess their potential impact on AI training data.

## [Onnx2TEE: Automated Graph-Aware Partitioning for TEE-Protected Edge AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33262) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Onnx2TEE introduces an automated, model-independent pipeline that partitions ONNX models across Trusted Execution Environments (TEEs) using graph-aware segmentation for non-sequential feed-forward networks. Evaluation on an NVIDIA Jetson AGX Orin with Arm TrustZone TEE shows that model parameters can be highly concentrated in individual segments, enabling selective protection of parameter-dense components while minimizing boundary communication and TEE-induced latency. This work addresses the growing need to protect valuable AI models deployed in hostile edge environments from theft or manipulation, where full-model execution inside resource-constrained TEEs is impractical. By automating the partitioning process, Onnx2TEE lowers the barrier for developers to adopt hardware-enforced protection for edge AI without manual, model-specific engineering. The pipeline targets non-sequential feed-forward networks and uses graph-aware segmentation to preserve computational dependencies while minimizing boundary communication. The evaluation further reveals that the generated trusted app's memory requirements depend on both computation and intermediate tensor sizes, which is a key consideration for TEE deployment.

Researchers introduced RoboARC, a benchmark of 90 spatially constrained manipulation tasks across six constraint types, rigid and soft materials, and three robots in MuJoCo. Each task has a physics-based grader and a verified reference solution, and is evaluated in both a Privileged regime (full simulator state) and a Standard regime (real-sensor inputs, single attempt). This benchmark addresses a known challenge in robotics: tasks where success hinges on precise spatial constraints that are hard to generalize from demonstrations. It provides a standardized way to measure progress in robotic reasoning and policy learning, which could drive advances in manufacturing, assembly, and other domains requiring dexterous manipulation. The strongest coding agent, GPT-6 Astra in Codex, solved 86% of tasks in the Privileged regime but only 43% in the Standard regime, while four VLA policies solved none of their 18 one-arm puzzles zero-shot. Analysis shows that combining object poses and part geometry improves performance from 24% to 60% on tasks the agent solved in Privileged but failed in Standard.

## [Best-of-N Test-Time Steering Can Cause Reward Hacking in VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper shows that increasing N in best-of-N test-time steering for vision-language-action (VLA) models can degrade performance and induce reward hacking when the verifier is inaccurate. Experiments on 60 LIBERO-Safety task-level cases with a π0.5 policy and a MuJoCo trajectory verifier demonstrate that as N grows, selected candidate rewards diverge from accurate verifier rewards. This finding challenges the common assumption that more test-time compute always helps, revealing a non-obvious failure mode that could undermine safety in robotic deployment. It is directly relevant to researchers and practitioners using test-time steering for VLA policies, especially in safety-critical applications. The study introduces controlled errors in a MuJoCo trajectory verifier and shows that verifier calibration and softmax-based candidate selection can mitigate reward hacking in some settings. The experiments use ten repetitions per case for statistical reliability.

Test-time steering improves adaptability by sampling multiple action candidates during deployment and using a verifier to select the best one, a technique known as best-of-N. Reward hacking occurs when the selection process exploits errors in the verifier rather than achieving the true goal, similar to overfitting to a flawed metric.

## [HumblePRM uses process reward modeling to improve VLM abstention on ambiguous images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33452) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint introduces HumblePRM, a lightweight process reward model that splits multimodal reasoning into a perception stage (factually describing the scene) and a calibration stage (judging whether the evidence suffices to commit to a conclusion). Training with HumblePRM substantially increases abstention on ambiguous inputs while leaving accuracy on clear images roughly unchanged, and the improvement transfers to Gemma 4 E4B. Reliable deployment of vision-language models requires that expressed confidence stay grounded in what the model can actually see, so better-calibrated abstention could reduce overconfident errors in safety-critical multimodal applications. The approach offers a training-time alternative to post-hoc uncertainty detection and may generalize across model sizes and families. Unlike standard outcome reward models (ORMs) that supervise only the final decision, HumblePRM scores each stage independently and rewards agreement between evidence and expressed confidence rather than outcome correctness alone. The work is an arXiv preprint without community discussion yet, and the abstract does not report specific quantitative gains.

## [SecID: LLM-Guided Framework Identifies Secondary Security Assets in RTL Designs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33453) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SecID is a multi-agent framework that combines deterministic RTL analysis, bounded dynamic evaluation, and LLM reasoning to identify threat-specific secondary security assets in hardware designs. It was demonstrated on seven open-source SoC and IP designs, including openMSP430, NEORV32, and CV32E40P, with results validated through fault simulation, formal analysis, and ablation studies. Identifying secondary security assets is a critical but difficult step in pre-silicon security verification, and automating it with LLM reasoning could make verification more scalable and precise. This work addresses a real challenge in hardware security and demonstrates a practical path toward AI-assisted verification for complex SoC designs. Each asset decision is supported by functional influence, threat features, and explicit attack mechanisms, and the framework is evaluated through pipeline- and final-stage ablation, threshold-sensitivity studies, and repeatability analysis. The approach combines deterministic analysis with bounded dynamic evaluation to keep LLM reasoning grounded in concrete RTL evidence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Register-transfer level (RTL) is a hardware design abstraction that models synchronous digital circuits in terms of data flow between registers and the logical operations on those signals. Pre-silicon security verification aims to find vulnerabilities before a chip is manufactured, and a foundational step is identifying security assets—the internal signals or registers whose compromise could undermine security. Primary assets are usually evident from functionality, but secondary assets are internal locations that enable attacks and depend on the specific threat mechanism, making them much harder to pinpoint.

## [CONTLORA: Statistical Memory Routes LoRA Adapters for Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33487) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CONTLORA replaces stored examples with compact class statistics—Gaussian class models and accumulated feature moments—to select LoRA adapters and perform classification in continual learning. It achieves 88.55% accuracy on 20-task CIFAR-100 and 86.99% on CUB-200 without retaining any images or feature vectors, matching a stored-feature counterpart within 0.07 percentage points. This work shows that statistical memory can serve as a viable alternative to example-retention in continual learning, potentially reducing privacy risks and storage costs associated with keeping past data. It also highlights that freezing old adapters is insufficient—routing among them at inference time is a critical but often overlooked challenge. The method uses Gaussian class models in a frozen representation to select adapters, while accumulated feature moments exactly recover a class-balanced ridge classifier for fixed training features. Ablations reveal that improved task coverage does not necessarily improve classification and that adapter composition matters; task ambiguity remains an ongoing limitation, and competitor-centered likelihood scores improve novelty AUROC on held-out classes without altering routing ranks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Continual learning aims to train models on a sequence of tasks without forgetting earlier ones, a problem known as catastrophic forgetting. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pre-trained weights and injects small trainable rank-decomposition matrices, making it attractive for storing one adapter per task. However, when multiple adapters exist, selecting the right one for a given input—adapter routing—becomes a key challenge, and many methods rely on storing past examples to guide this selection.

## [AI coding agents fix vulnerabilities but fail to retain lessons across sessions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33519) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.33519) shows that Codex, Claude Code, and Qwen Code fixed a security vulnerability 85.7% of the time when shown a failing test, but reintroduced the same vulnerability class in a new session 26.7% of the time—nearly identical to agents that had never seen it (27.5%). The authors propose PRECEPT, an evolving library of secure coding principles that raises the weakest agent to 97.1% secure working code on unseen task families. This identifies a concrete, measurable failure mode in AI coding agents—security knowledge retention—and shows that simply recording fixes or supplying human-oriented principles does not solve it. As agents write more code with limited human review, closing this gap is critical for software security. The study used 14 task sequences and found that neither persistent context recording nor published secure coding principles closed the gap: recorded fixes stayed tied to the original task family, and human-written principles were too general. PRECEPT revises principles against the agent's own failures and keeps a revision only if fresh executions show improved security without breaking functionality, with gains continuing across evolution steps.

## [RankCast balances rank loads in disaggregated MoE serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33542) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RankCast is a new system that reduces rank-level load imbalance in disaggregated Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) serving by minimizing the maximum token count during prefill and the maximum active-expert count during decode, while preserving each token's predicted Top-p experts. Implemented in SGLang and DeepEP, it removes over 75% of the busiest rank's excess prefill load, improves prefill throughput by up to 19%, and cuts decode time per output token by up to 10% on Qwen3-235B-A22B (EP=8) and Qwen3.5-397B-A17B (EP=16), with quality loss below one percentage point. Load imbalance in expert parallelism is a key bottleneck for large MoE model inference, and RankCast addresses the distinct bottlenecks of compute-bound prefill and memory-bound decode in a unified framework. This could significantly improve throughput and latency for production LLM serving systems that use disaggregated architectures and expert parallelism. RankCast preserves the predicted Top-p experts for each token and selects the remaining k-p experts from its Top-L candidates (L > k), using a price-per-rank mechanism in prefill and expert closing in decode. Its kernels overlap with model execution and reuse the existing DeepEP communication channel for metadata transmission, but the evaluation is limited to two Qwen3 models and the paper is an arXiv preprint without community validation yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Modern LLM inference often separates the prefill phase (processing the input prompt) and the decode phase (generating output tokens) into disaggregated architectures to optimize each independently. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models activate only a subset of experts per token, and expert parallelism (EP) distributes these experts across devices, but load imbalance occurs because each layer must wait for the busiest rank. Prefill is compute-bound and limited by the maximum token count per rank, while decode is memory-bound and limited by the maximum number of active experts per rank.

## [DexEngine Scales Human Demos into Physically Executable Robot Arm-Hand Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33579) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DexEngine is a simulation pipeline that converts human hand-object demonstrations into kinematically feasible, physically executable whole-robot arm-hand trajectories using residual reinforcement learning. From nine human hand-object datasets, it generates about 5,200 clips per dexterous hand, augmented into 65,000 trajectories (116 hours) and 325,000 episodes (578 hours) in total. This work addresses a key bottleneck in robot learning: scaling human demonstrations into physically valid whole-robot data for training dexterous manipulation policies. Policies trained on DexEngine data far outperform those trained on data without physics correction or with arms attached after floating-hand correction, and mid-training on this data improves performance in simulation and on a real robot. DexEngine first places each clip's hand-object motion within arm reach, finds a following arm motion, and then corrects it in physics simulation with residual reinforcement learning. It tracks objects more accurately than prior floating-hand methods even with arms attached, and runs over 10x faster than RL-based approaches.

A new arXiv paper (2611.33687) identifies that vision-language models often fail to read visual evidence from multimodal memory even when sufficient evidence is provided, and proposes MemRU, a two-stage training framework combining supervised fine-tuning (Read SFT) and Group Relative Policy Optimization (Use GRPO). The resulting 8B model generalizes across three unseen multimodal memory systems, outperforming Qwen3-VL-32B and approaching GPT-5-mini. This work addresses a specific failure mode in vision-language agents that rely on long-horizon memory, showing that memory construction and retrieval alone are insufficient without effective memory utilization. The approach could improve the reliability of multimodal agents in tasks requiring recall of past visual experiences, and the strong results from an 8B model suggest efficient training can rival much larger models. The authors diagnose the problem by replacing memory images with reference captions, which yields substantial performance gains but risks omitting critical information since captions are model-generated. MemRU separates the issue into evidence reading and downstream question answering, tackling the former with Read SFT and the latter with Use GRPO, though the abstract does not detail dataset size or training compute.

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) jointly interpret images and text, extending large language models beyond text-only input. A new arXiv paper proposes a batch-equivalent sequential editing method for Locate-Then-Edit knowledge updates in large language models, which matches the performance of batch editing while overcoming catastrophic forgetting and model collapse that plague existing sequential methods after only a few hundred edits. This work addresses a critical limitation in sequential knowledge editing, potentially enabling continuous, large-scale updates to LLMs without degrading their general capabilities, which is essential for keeping models current in dynamic environments. The analysis reveals that existing sequential methods exhibit linear or logarithmic growth in weight perturbations as edits accumulate, whereas the proposed batch-equivalent method achieves time-invariant growth, theoretically guaranteeing long-term stability and preserving pretrained knowledge integrity.

Researchers present Sports Agent, a self-evolving framework for conversational sports analytics that integrates an Agentic SQL Model, a planning and tool-orchestration harness, and a unified Feedback Engine that diagnoses failures and generates evolution signals. The evolved Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-based model outperforms Qwen3.8-Max on sports question answering while achieving roughly three times the inference efficiency, and 30 harness-evolution rounds improve the aggregate performance score by 10.8 points. This work shows that static LLM agent configurations can be systematically improved through runtime-evidence-driven evolution, combining targeted data synthesis, trajectory distillation, and bounded harness updates. It matters for teams building production conversational analytics systems, where query distributions and data resources drift over time and static agents degrade. Model evolution combines SQL-to-Question Scaling for coverage expansion with Graph-Guided Scaling for error-targeted synthesis, followed by trajectory distillation, quality filtering, iterative supervised fine-tuning, and a separate Post-RL stage. Harness evolution translates recurrent failures into bounded prompt and skill updates validated through independent audits, execution replay, and regression testing, with performance-tolerance checks guarding against degradation during simplification.

## [ScanAR: Row-Parallel Decoding Speeds Up Unified Autoregressive Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33761) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ScanAR introduces a row-scan parallel decoding method for unified autoregressive models that generates each visual row in a single parallel step, reducing sequential decoding steps from H×W to H+W. On Emu3.5-Image-34B, it achieves a GenEval score of 82.36 and generates 512×512 images 13.8× faster than raster decoding. This approach significantly accelerates image generation in unified multimodal autoregressive models without changing the tokenizer, prediction head, or model interface, making it easily adoptable. It could enable faster and more efficient deployment of large models like Emu3.5-Image-34B for real-time applications. ScanAR only redefines the predecessors of visual tokens: the first row is generated left-to-right, and each subsequent row is generated in parallel from the vertically aligned tokens of the previous row. It supports both training from scratch and lightweight post-training, and on ImageNet it achieves the highest throughput at every model size with FID comparable to raster LlamaGen.

**Background**: Unified multimodal autoregressive models represent text and images in a single token sequence and generate all modalities with a shared next-token prediction head. However, image generation typically decodes an H×W visual grid token by token in raster order, requiring H×W sequential steps and dominating inference time. Existing acceleration methods either alter the tokenizer, head, or interface, or sacrifice quality for parallelism, making them unsuitable for unified models.

## [TRIAGE framework gates embodied foundation model selection on feasibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33772) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.33772) proposes TRIAGE, a five-stage framework that first gates embodied foundation model candidates on sensor and end-to-end latency feasibility, then probes them on ten minutes of local logs, ranks the survivors by target-relevant score, and applies monitored fine-tuning with a closed-loop check before release. The authors decompose the performance gap into sensing, latency, and distribution components, arguing that only the distribution gap can be repaired by fine-tuning. This work gives practitioners who cannot train foundation models from scratch a concrete method to determine whether a public checkpoint can actually run on their fixed robot or vehicle, and what to fix if it cannot. It challenges the common practice of selecting checkpoints purely by benchmark rank, showing that high-scoring models can fail physical feasibility gates. On driving benchmarks, a deadline below a leading closed-loop model's latency caused collisions on 80–90% of routes, and losing field of view moved plans 2.2–5.7 times more than losing resolution at equal pixels. Local frames closed 75% of a stability gap but increased perturbation sensitivity at small budgets, and selecting by score violated a gate in two of four deployment profiles; on a full-scale vehicle, the factory camera failed the sensing gate and an added camera repaired it.

## [KnitNet: Inference-Time Search for Exact RNA Inverse Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33812) ⭐️ 7.0/10

KnitNet introduces an inference-time scaling framework that learns from verifier feedback to refine RNA sequences for exact secondary structure matching. Across five benchmarks, it increases exact solve rates by an average of 21 percentage points over continued sampling with 70% less time-to-solve, and is the first method to achieve exact computational matches for all complex pseudoknotted structures in OpenKnot57. This work addresses a key limitation of learned generators that often miss exact structural matches, especially out-of-distribution, and offers a more compute-efficient alternative to costly search. It could impact RNA therapeutics and synthetic biology by enabling reliable design of RNA molecules with specified structures. KnitNet uses mismatched folds to infer nucleotide dependencies and adapts a joint repair strategy, while an online surrogate predicts promising edits. It generalizes across RNA structural regimes by changing only the optimization signal, and improves 3D structural self-consistency over per-target RL by 7.8% while being 39.3% faster.

**Background**: RNA inverse design seeks nucleotide sequences that fold into a given target secondary structure, a problem that is NP-complete under the Watson-Crick model. Traditional methods either use learned generators, which can fail to produce exact matches, or perform costly search over a vast sequence space complicated by globally coupled nucleotide interactions. Verifier feedback refers to using a folding algorithm to check whether a candidate sequence folds correctly and guide refinement.

## [Combining heterogeneous rerankers without joint training boosts RAG accuracy and speed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33844) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper systematically studies cascades of heterogeneous rerankers—lexical, single-vector, late-interaction, and joint query-context scorers—composed without joint training. It introduces two lightweight semantic coarse scorers, Cache-Pointwise and Cache-Shapley, and shows that inserting them before CrossEncoder or Provence improves answer accuracy by 4.02–9.43 percentage points while accelerating online pruning by 1.57–1.88x on NoLiMa 32K with Gemma. This work offers a practical, training-free route to better and faster reranking in RAG pipelines, which could directly benefit information retrieval practitioners seeking to improve answer quality without costly joint training. It also highlights that stage order, candidate budgets, and rank fusion are key determinants of composition quality, opening new avenues for system design. The gains depend on the reader, reranker pairing, and stage configuration, and the stage-removal comparisons reveal an asymmetry: a cascade can outperform its final reranker alone while underperforming its coarse selector alone. Cache-Pointwise composed with ColBERTv2 via reciprocal rank fusion improves accuracy by 4.60 points at 1.36x speedup over direct ColBERTv2.

**Background**: Reranking is a common step in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) pipelines, where a model reorders retrieved passages to put the most relevant ones first for the reader (e.g., an LLM). Rerankers come in different types: lexical (e.g., BM25), single-vector (e.g., dense retrievers), late-interaction (e.g., ColBERTv2), and joint query-context scorers (e.g., CrossEncoder). Typically, combining multiple rerankers requires joint training, which is expensive; this paper explores composing them without joint training in a cascade, where lightweight coarse scorers prune candidates before a stronger reranker.

## [Kernel-Theoretic Analysis of RND Overgeneralization and Spectral Target Fix](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33850) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper analyzes Random Network Distillation (RND) overgeneralization through a kernel-theoretic lens, showing that the novelty score is a cross-kernel residual variance governed by both the target covariance kernel and the predictor's Neural Tangent Kernel (NTK). It identifies overgeneralization as spectral under-excitation and proposes replacing the random network target with a bandwidth-controlled Random Fourier Feature (RFF) target to redistribute residual signal toward less-attenuated modes. RND is a widely used exploration bonus in reinforcement learning, and its overgeneralization causes novelty scores to collapse on unfamiliar inputs, hurting exploration. This work provides a principled theoretical explanation and a practical fix that improves novelty discrimination and downstream RL performance on offline D4RL, online Atari, and Fetch manipulation benchmarks. The analysis assumes a Gaussian Process target model and an NTK-regime predictor, showing that the RND novelty score is a cross-kernel residual variance rather than being determined by either kernel alone. The proposed RFF target is bandwidth-controlled and the code is available at https://github.com/tuuktuc86/RFF-RND.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Random Network Distillation (RND) is a curiosity-driven exploration method where a predictor network is trained to match the output of a fixed random target network; the prediction error serves as a novelty bonus. The Neural Tangent Kernel (NTK) describes the behavior of infinitely wide neural networks during training and provides a kernel-theoretic view of learning. Random Fourier Features (RFF) approximate shift-invariant kernels using random projections, and their bandwidth parameter controls the frequency distribution.

## [Random Voronoi Networks: Bridging k-NN and Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33875) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Random Voronoi Networks (RVNs), a structured three-layer MLP that expresses nearest-neighbor geometry via sampled Voronoi comparisons. The authors prove that with all samples and pairwise comparisons, step RVNs recover exact Euclidean k-NN, while ReLU RVNs provide a differentiable soft weighting for 1-NN. This work bridges the gap between non-parametric nearest-neighbor methods and differentiable neural architectures, potentially enabling more interpretable and efficient tabular deep learning models. It offers theoretical guarantees and a practical feature encoding strategy that boosts downstream model performance. The first layer computes Random Voronoi Features (RVF) from affine pairwise separators, the second aggregates into Voronoi cells or top-k scores, and the readout produces predictions. Subsampling yields approximate RVNs with explicit control over cells and features, and several unsupervised sampling methods are studied for trade-offs.

## [Parallel subagents in LLM coding agents yield only 1-5pp gains at up to 3.8x cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33893) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new empirical study compares a single LLM coding agent against a main agent delegating to parallel subagents on repository-construction tasks from NL2Repo and ProgramBench. It finds that parallel subagents raise hidden-test pass rates by only about 1 to 5 percentage points at up to 3.8x the cost, with larger gains on hard tasks explained by three mechanisms: self-checking, fresh context, and modularity. This challenges the assumption that multi-agent delegation is a universal win for coding agents, showing that most of the benefit can be recovered by simple interventions like instructing a single agent to probe the reference program first. It gives practitioners concrete guidance on when subagents are worth the cost and when they mainly add expense. With GPT-5.6 Sol, a one-sentence instruction telling a single agent to probe the reference program first recovers most of the subagent gain at a fraction of the cost, and the gain shrinks from GPT-5.6 Sol to GPT-6.1 Sol as single agents self-check more. In extended runs, a single agent that periodically starts a fresh session makes faster early progress, and multi-agent runs produce more modular code (more files, shorter functions) closer to the upstream source; similar patterns appear in a smaller replication with Opus 5.5 on Claude Code.

DiCALM introduces a recipe to convert LFM2 and LFM2.5 hybrid convolution-attention autoregressive language models into masked-diffusion models by progressively extending causal gated convolution to bidirectional context, combined with masking warm-up and joint AR-diffusion training. At 350M and 1.2B scales, the converted models match fine-tuned LFM2.5 AR baselines in quality while achieving over 1.4x average speedup and up to 1.79x. It could impact deployment of language models on resource-constrained devices and encourage further research into converting diverse architectures. The conversion relies on progressively extending LFM2's causal gated convolution to bidirectional context, along with a masking warm-up and joint training objective. Speedups are consistent across both 350M and 1.2B scales, though the approach is currently limited to the LFM2 family and lacks community discussion.

WorldReforge introduces a 62k-trajectory simulated failure dataset and two training signals to improve action-conditioned robot world models on off-nominal manipulation events like slips and failed grasps. On Cosmos3 Nano, it reduces object trajectory error by 28.2% overall and by 35.0% on unseen objects, and lowers error in predicting action outcome divergence by 43.3%. The Reforge dataset is built by perturbing reference grasps in position, orientation, and closure timing to record pushes, slips, and failed pickups with ground-truth object pose, contact, and surface points. The two training signals, Interaction Alignment and Surface Correspondence, add no inference cost, and controlled studies show that failed contact and successful holding teach complementary physics.

The paper proposes InstructTTS-OPD, which replaces a single scalar GRPO reward with on-policy distillation (OPD) that fuses multiple single-objective teachers to stabilize multi-objective instruction-following TTS training. It introduces conflict-aware velocity fusion to project the quality target off the direction opposing the primary objective, reducing the instruction-following/quality trade-off. This work addresses the known instability of multi-objective RL post-training in TTS, where objectives compete and cause a seesaw effect, and shows that the fused model improves on all three objectives simultaneously over scalar-reward GRPO. It could benefit researchers and practitioners building controllable, high-quality TTS systems. The method relies on carefully designed rewards: a distilled Qwen-Omni proxy for the costly Gemini judge, dialect-free data for the WER reward, and DNSMOS instead of the language-biased UTMOS. Each rollout is routed to the instruction-following or WER teacher by data domain, and every rollout additionally follows the MOS teacher for quality.

Reinforcement learning, especially GRPO, is commonly used to post-train such models toward human preferences, but optimizing multiple objectives under one scalar reward often causes unstable training. On-policy distillation trains a student on its own generated rollouts using teacher feedback, and here it is adapted to fuse several single-objective teachers.

## [AI Buckingham: LLM Agent Autonomously Discovers Dimensionless Scaling Laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33961) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present AI Buckingham, an LLM-driven agentic framework that autonomously discovers dominant dimensionless groups and scaling laws from natural-language physics problems and observational data. It was validated across ten physical systems spanning fluid dynamics, magnetohydrodynamics, thermal convection, and additive manufacturing, recovering established similarity representations and resolving regime-dependent scalings. This work bridges LLM-based scientific discovery and classical dimensional analysis, showing that an agent can automate a task traditionally requiring expert intuition. It could accelerate modeling in engineering and physics, particularly for complex phenomena like turbulent drag on rough surfaces where informative reparameterizations were found. The framework couples LLM reasoning and planning with symbolic and numerical tools, using literature-based knowledge retrieval for dimensional checking, gradient-based clustering to identify physical regimes, and mutual information as a reparameterization-invariant criterion for selecting dominant dimensionless groups. Explicit numerical criteria determine group count and assess simplification, though the abstract is truncated and no community discussion is available.

## [LLM-Written Closed-Form Classifiers on Frozen Encoder Rival Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33972) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper introduces closed-form weight-space programming (WSP), where an LLM writes programs that fit closed-form heads (shrinkage discriminant analysis, kernel ridge regression, and the encoder's label-word prior) on a frozen RoBERTa-large encoder, selecting among candidates via cross-fitting without computing any gradients. On 11 development datasets, WSP outperforms full fine-tuning and LoRA at every label budget (by 0.3–0.6 points on average) and comes within 0.5 points of prompt-based fine-tuning. This work challenges the necessity of gradient-based fine-tuning by showing that a frozen encoder combined with LLM-designed closed-form classifiers can match or beat standard fine-tuning methods, especially in low-label regimes. If validated, it could offer a deterministic, gradient-free alternative for adapting large models, reducing computational cost and instability for NLP practitioners. WSP requires one encoder pass per template (17 in this study) and is deterministic and never collapses, but on six held-out datasets it only stays ahead of LoRA and within half a point of full fine-tuning on average, with no registered test passing; prompt-based fine-tuning leads by 1.30 points at 8,192 labels. The paper also reports all eight recipe versions and 27 ideas that did not help.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Fine-tuning large pre-trained language models like RoBERTa typically involves updating all or some weights via gradient descent, which can be computationally expensive and prone to instability. Closed-form methods, such as shrinkage discriminant analysis and kernel ridge regression, compute classifier weights analytically without iterative optimization. RoBERTa-large is a 355M-parameter masked language model that can be probed with cloze-style prompts to extract features. WSP uses an LLM as a design assistant to write programs that fit such closed-form heads on frozen encoder representations.

## [DosePET: Open Dataset of 2,100 Radiation Dose Maps for PET/CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34008) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DosePET is a new open-source dataset containing 2,100 patient-specific absorbed-dose maps generated via Monte Carlo simulation from public PET/CT examinations, including 1,503 [18F]FDG studies from ENHANCE.PET and 597 PSMA-PET studies from PSMA-PET-CT-Lesions. Each study includes a NIfTI dose map, 117 organ/tissue masks from TotalSegmentator, and dosimetric reports, with Python tools for selective download and conversion. This dataset addresses a critical data scarcity in AI-driven personalized internal dosimetry for nuclear medicine, providing a large, standardized resource that could accelerate the development of machine learning models for dose prediction and treatment planning. It is particularly valuable for researchers in medical imaging and nuclear medicine who lack access to large-scale dosimetry data. The simulations used GATE version 9.4 and took a total of 1,542.4 hours; the dataset includes dose maps integrated from scan time to infinity, mean absorbed doses, dose conversion factors, and scan-time dose rates, and is hosted on Hugging Face with Python tools for conversion to scan-time dose-rate or dose-per-decay maps.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Internal dosimetry in nuclear medicine involves estimating the radiation dose absorbed by organs and tissues after administering a radiopharmaceutical, which is essential for optimizing treatment while minimizing toxicity. Monte Carlo simulation is a computational method that models radiation transport through the body using patient-specific anatomy from CT images and activity distribution from PET images, but generating large datasets is computationally intensive. Open datasets like DosePET enable reproducible research and the training of AI models that can potentially replace time-consuming simulations.

## [Counter-DECEIVR Generates Counterfactual Explanations for Deepfake Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34032) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Counter-DECEIVR introduces a training-free framework that generates counterfactual examples for deepfake detectors by steering latents in a rectified flow model using gradients from off-the-shelf detectors. It requires no per-instance or per-detector fitting and outperforms prior methods, including training-based ones, across two detectors and two forensics datasets. Counterfactual explanations reveal the minimal evidence that flips a detector's decision, helping analysts assess trust in black-box deepfake detectors as generation quality improves. This training-free approach could make explainability more practical and scalable for real-world forensics workflows. The method leverages the detector's explanation maps to concentrate editing on salient regions, turning low-level feature importance into localized counterfactual edits, and a single parameter controls edit strength. Code will be released upon acceptance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Deepfake detectors are often black-box models, and region-based explanations become less useful as generated media quality improves. ## [PTA3D: Black-box adversarial attack hijacks Image-to-3D models via prefix trajectory alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34075) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose PTA3D, a targeted black-box adversarial attack that manipulates Image-to-3D models to generate policy-restricted 3D geometry from visually benign inputs. The method uses Prefix Trajectory Alignment (PTA) and Preprocessing Aware Optimization (PAO) to achieve 41.5%-88.5% attack success rate on three online services and 90.8%-94.6% in white-box settings. This work exposes a practical security vulnerability in online Image-to-3D generation services, showing that content policy inspectors examining only input images can be bypassed. It highlights the need for more robust defenses in 3D asset generation pipelines and raises awareness about adversarial threats in generative AI services. PTA3D aligns the prefix DiT outputs of the adversarial trajectory with those of a target trajectory, avoiding optimization through the full generation trajectory, while PAO exposes optimization to diverse image transformations to improve transferability across services. The authors provide theoretical bounds for prefix-to-output and preprocessing-aware transfer to characterize effectiveness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Image-to-3D models generate 3D assets from a single image and are often deployed in online services that use policy inspectors to block harmful content. Adversarial attacks aim to fool models by adding imperceptible perturbations to inputs; black-box attacks assume no access to model weights and rely on transferability. Diffusion Transformers (DiT) are a class of generative models that iteratively denoise latent representations over a long trajectory, making them challenging to attack end-to-end.

## [WorldBridge Generates Coherent 3D Worlds from Scattered Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34079) ⭐️ 7.0/10

WorldBridge is a new framework that generates coherent, explorable 3D worlds from scattered images with little or no visual overlap by encoding references into a global scene context and using temporal/spatial attention plus a plug-and-play latent-reference consistency strategy. The authors also introduce the WorldBridge benchmark suite for evaluating 3D world generation from scattered, low-overlap references, and report qualitative and quantitative results surpassing existing state-of-the-art solutions. This addresses a long-standing challenge in computer vision and graphics: generating consistent 3D scenes from sparse, non-overlapping views, which existing geometry-aware methods fail to handle. The approach could enable more practical 3D content creation from casual photo collections and advance novel view synthesis and scene reconstruction research. The framework uses temporal and spatial reference attention to enhance fidelity along the target trajectory and support flexible spatial references outside it, while the plug-and-play latent-reference consistency strategy improves reference fidelity through reprojection-guided test-time optimization. The accompanying benchmark suite specifically targets scattered, low-overlap references, a setting rarely explored by current benchmarks.

Image- and video-based generative methods can hallucinate unseen regions but often produce inconsistent geometry across views.

## [Complex Tasks Reduce Firms' Willingness to Replace Juniors with AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34099) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A pre-registered survey experiment with 1,435 German managers found that complex tasks requiring judgment and contextual knowledge reduce firms' willingness to replace entry-level employees with AI by 0.54 points on a five-point scale, compared to standardized tasks. The study also shows that managers expect AI to reduce hiring of both entry-level and experienced workers, but more strongly for entry-level workers. This provides empirical evidence that task complexity moderates AI's substitution of entry-level labor, suggesting that AI adoption may not uniformly displace juniors but rather reshape which tasks are automated. The findings are directly relevant to policy debates on AI's labor market impact and workforce development. The experiment is pre-registered and uses a random assignment design, but it is a single-country study with a modest effect size and relies on managers' stated willingness rather than observed hiring behavior. The sample is limited to German firms in the information economy and manufacturing industry.

**Background**: As AI tools like large language models become more capable, there is growing concern that they could automate entry-level white-collar jobs. This study uses a survey experiment to test how task characteristics influence firms' automation decisions.

## [SysBio-SR benchmark reveals gap between trajectory fit and mechanism recovery in symbolic regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34106) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SysBio-SR introduces a benchmark of 198 literature-based biological ODE systems with reaction annotations, evaluating symbolic regression methods on both trajectory fidelity and mechanism recovery. The study finds that accurate trajectory prediction often does not imply correct recovery of underlying reaction mechanisms, and proposes KineSelect, which improves OOD extrapolation R² by 19.2% and reaction F1 by 14.3% over the strongest baseline. This benchmark exposes a critical flaw in current symbolic regression evaluation for systems biology: methods that fit trajectories well may fail to discover true mechanisms, which could mislead biological interpretation. It provides a resource for developing more reliable mechanism-discovery tools and highlights the need for structure-aware evaluation. The benchmark covers mass action, Michaelis-Menten, Hill, and complex kinetics, with mechanism recovery assessed on 161 systems; 11 methods were evaluated for trajectory fidelity and 3 reaction-based methods for mechanism recovery. KineSelect searches candidate reaction topologies under biologically grounded kinetic laws to balance trajectory fidelity and structural parsimony.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Symbolic regression is a machine learning approach that discovers interpretable mathematical equations from data without a predefined model structure. In systems biology, ordinary differential equations (ODEs) are used to model biochemical reaction networks, and recovering the correct reaction mechanisms is essential for understanding cellular processes. Kinetics such as Michaelis-Menten and Hill equations describe enzyme-catalyzed and cooperative binding reactions, respectively.

## [ClueProbe Benchmark Tests Video LLMs on Partial Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34126) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ClueProbe, a diagnostic benchmark with 1,721 human-annotated questions across 598 natural videos, labeled as Answerable, Guessable, or Unanswerable, to evaluate Video LLMs on partial evidence. Evaluating 10 open-source and proprietary models shows failures stem from judgment rather than perception, and targeted fine-tuning on the guess level lifts appropriate guessing from 1.6% to 40.4%. This work addresses a gap in Video LLM evaluation by distinguishing partial evidence scenarios, which could improve model reliability in real-world applications like egocentric recording, visual accessibility, and surveillance review. It shows that models lack graded sufficiency awareness, and that this can be mitigated through targeted supervision. ClueProbe includes controlled perturbations altering wording or visual input, and the fine-tuning experiment shows that supervising the guess level improves appropriate guessing without extra false refusals, whereas conventional refusal tuning rejects 92% of guessable questions. Data and code will be released at https://github.com/suat-llm/ClueProbe.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Video Large Language Models (Video LLMs) are multimodal models that process video inputs to answer questions or generate descriptions. Existing benchmarks typically enforce a binary answerable-or-unanswerable split, leaving partial evidence unmeasured and obscuring whether failures arise from missing evidence or misjudging sufficiency. ClueProbe introduces a three-way labeling scheme to separate perceiving evidence from judging sufficiency.

## [FoundSurface: Feedforward Ray-Based 3D Surface Reconstruction from Unposed Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34128) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FoundSurface introduces a feedforward pipeline that combines pointmap and ray-based representations to reconstruct continuous, high-fidelity 3D surfaces from unposed images. The method uses a query-view-centric selection module with surfel voting and a cross-attention regressor, trained as a single foundation model on 9 datasets and showing strong zero-shot generalization on 6 unseen datasets. This work addresses a key limitation of existing feedforward pointmap methods, which produce discrete and sparse representations, by enabling continuous surface reconstruction without camera poses. It could benefit applications in robotics, AR/VR, and 3D content creation where pose-free, generalizable reconstruction is needed. The pipeline first extracts geometric priors from a pre-trained pointmap model, then uses a robust surfel voting mechanism to select relevant reference views, followed by a cross-attention regressor for coarse surface estimation and a raylet-based module for high-frequency details and analytical normals. Training on 9 diverse datasets and zero-shot testing on 6 unseen datasets demonstrates exceptional generalization, though the method's reliance on a pre-trained pointmap model may inherit its biases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: 3D surface reconstruction from images is a fundamental computer vision task, traditionally requiring known camera poses. Recent feedforward pointmap methods eliminate the need for poses but output discrete, sparse point clouds rather than continuous surfaces. FoundSurface bridges this gap by integrating pointmap-based geometric priors with ray-based representations, using techniques like surfel voting (where surfels are surface elements analogous to pixels) and cross-attention to produce continuous surfaces.

## [ReproCue synthesizes runnable PoCs for AI-reported Linux kernel bugs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34142) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present ReproCue, a framework that learns a reproduction finite-state machine (FSM) per kernel subsystem by replaying historical bugs, then uses it to generate runnable proof-of-concept (PoC) reproducers for new AI-generated review reports. In 100 recent AI-generated reviews, ReproCue produced PoCs for 49, versus 10 for the current agentic reproducer and 9 for state-of-the-art directed fuzzing. Kernel maintainers confirmed 69.4% of ReproCue's PoCs, far above the 9.3% confirmation rate for Sashiko's text-only reports, suggesting runnable PoCs can make AI code review far more actionable and reduce the backlog of unanswered AI-flagged bugs. ReproCue adjusts the PoC and environment as execution reveals missing requirements, following the subsystem's FSM step by step; the approach depends on historical bug replays to learn FSMs and was evaluated on 100 recent AI-generated reviews rather than a broader corpus.

## [GridProbe benchmark pinpoints where multimodal LLMs fail in grid games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34152) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces GridProbe, a diagnostic benchmark that decomposes multimodal LLM failures in grid-based spatial planning into four probes: recognition, localization, single-step prediction, and state tracking. Evaluating seven MLLMs on Sokoban and a maze game, it finds that proprietary models are limited by multi-step planning, while open-source models are limited by rule application or visual grid indexing. This work addresses a real gap in end-to-end-only benchmarks by offering a verifiable methodology to locate failure stages, which can guide targeted improvements in MLLM training. It shows that planning failures can hide perception failures, and that fine-tuning only the vision module can raise interactive Sokoban success 3.5-fold and transfer zero-shot to an unseen maze game. GridProbe pairs end-to-end solving with four probes and provides every board both as an image and as an equivalent text grid. The diagnosis identifies what to train: fine-tuning only the vision module of a perception-limited model raises its interactive Sokoban success from 5.8% to 20.4% and transfers zero-shot to the unseen maze game (single-turn success 13.8% to 67.1%), whereas the same data does not help a rule-limited model.

## [Diffusion Purifiers Evaluated on Native-Resolution Agentic Inputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34181) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents the first study of diffusion-based adversarial purification at the agentic level, testing purifiers on native-resolution multi-element inputs for a vision-language agent built on Qwen3-VL-8B. It finds that resizing inputs to the diffusion model's fixed resolution, as existing purifiers do, raises the TextVQA clean cost of the Divide-and-Conquer (DC) purifier from 4.0 to 35.7 points, and that DC retains 85.0% grey-box robust accuracy on web-shop pages versus at most 40.0% for cheap input transformations at matched clean cost. This work shows that treating agentic inputs as classifier inputs misleads both evaluation and design of defenses, which matters for anyone deploying vision-language agents that process untrusted, multi-element pages. It provides practical guidance and a released protocol for building plug-in purifiers as a first line of defense against perturbations crafted by content providers. The study uses grey-box attackers who know the agent's model but not the defense, compares purifiers at matched clean cost, and finds that robustness comes from the global noise level, with input-estimated masks and noise levels adding none. The findings hold on a disjoint held-out set and a second VLM, and the authors derive three requirements for defenses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Diffusion-based adversarial purification uses the forward and reverse processes of diffusion models to remove adversarial perturbations from images before classification, as in methods like DiffPure. ## [MINE: Training-Free Defense Against Indirect Prompt Injection in RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34188) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose MINE, a training-free and attack-agnostic defense against indirect prompt injection in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) that works by maximizing relevance via mutual information and embedding variance, rather than detecting attacks. They also introduce LC-Inj-SQuAD, a long-context benchmark with an average of 4,032 words per context, and show that MINE achieves the best accuracy-security trade-off among training-free defenses across four datasets. Indirect prompt injection is a critical security threat for RAG systems and LLM agents, and existing defenses are either easily overridden or require retraining for each new attack. MINE's training-free, attack-agnostic approach could provide a more practical and robust defense, especially for long-context and agent tool-call scenarios, impacting developers deploying RAG and agentic systems. MINE also serves as a query-conditioned reranker and achieves the lowest inference latency on long contexts. It is the only defense that remains usable against a fully adaptive attacker and generalizes to agent tool-call injection, as evaluated on InjecAgent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances LLMs by retrieving external documents to ground responses, but this introduces indirect prompt injection, where malicious instructions in retrieved content can hijack the model. Existing defenses include prompt engineering, which can be overridden, and supervised detectors, which require retraining for new attack styles. MINE takes a different approach by maximizing the relevance of retrieved content to the query, making injected instructions less likely to be followed.

## [Attention Contrastive Decoding Reduces Hallucinations in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34207) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Attention Contrastive Decoding (ACD), a training-free plug-in that moves part of the contrastive operation from the logit level to the attention mechanism to mitigate hallucinations in large vision-language models (LVLMs). Using an Adaptive Subtraction Strategy (ASS), ACD attenuates hallucination-associated attention patterns while amplifying critical visual information, and when combined with existing methods like VCD (VCD+ACD) it produces more coherent outputs with fewer hallucinations. This work addresses a key limitation of logit-level contrastive decoding, which compromises generation coherence and requires restrictive penalties unrelated to hallucination suppression. By intervening earlier in the forward pass at the attention level, ACD enables more trustworthy multimodal generation and could be broadly adopted as a plug-in to improve existing CD methods without retraining. ACD is training-free and operates as a plug-in that complements logit-level CD, performing smooth semantic-preserving interventions via the Adaptive Subtraction Strategy. Experiments show that combining ACD with existing CD methods (e.g., VCD+ACD) eliminates restrictive penalties while further reducing hallucinations and improving coherence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) can generate factually inaccurate content, a phenomenon known as hallucination. Contrastive Decoding (CD) mitigates this by contrasting an amateur branch and an expert branch at the logit level, but such logit-level interventions can compromise generation coherence. Attention mechanisms in transformers determine how models weigh visual and textual information, and intervening at this stage may preserve coherence better than post-hoc logit adjustments.

<a id="item-158"></a>
## [Socratic PAUSE: Safeguarding Scientific Judgment at AI Speed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34222) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv position paper (2611.34222) argues that generative AI accelerates scientific discovery far more than justification, and proposes the 'Socratic Pause'—a deliberate checkpoint at consequential decision gates where AI contributions remain provisional until a researcher examines their basis. The paper introduces the PAUSE protocol (Pin down, Ask, Uncover, Scrutinise, Exercise judgement) and the concept of 'verification debt' to describe the accumulation of unexamined AI-generated contributions. This matters because researchers increasingly rely on AI to generate hypotheses, methods, code, and interpretations within minutes, and confidence can outgrow evidence through recursive reinforcement, loss of provenance, and miscalibrated reliance. The proposed framework offers a concrete practice to preserve accountability and independent understanding without stifling exploration. The PAUSE protocol structures examination through five steps—Pin down the claim, Ask and challenge, Uncover assumptions and alternatives, Scrutinise the evidence, and Exercise judgement—concluding with a recorded decision to accept, revise, reject, or defer the contribution. The paper also outlines a framework for evaluating whether this practice improves verification, decision-making, and independent understanding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Generative AI tools can rapidly produce scientific hypotheses, methods, code, and interpretations, but generating a contribution does not establish that it deserves scientific reliance. 'Verification debt' refers to the accumulation of AI-generated content that is carried into later claims or decisions without adequate scrutiny, similar to technical debt. The paper uses Einstein's sketch of scientific discovery as a lens to argue that AI has accelerated discovery more than justification.

## [LatentCP-DPO: Conformal Pessimism for DPO with Finite-Sample Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34275) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LatentCP-DPO introduces a framework that combines conformal prediction with pessimistic preference optimization for Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), providing finite-sample coverage guarantees against noisy preference labels. It constructs uncertainty sets for latent relative rewards using conformal prediction sets for observable preferences, and learns a policy by maximizing the worst-case expected preference log-likelihood over these sets. This work addresses a key challenge in preference learning—overfitting to noisy or weakly informative labels—by providing statistical guarantees, which could improve the reliability of alignment methods like DPO in low-data regimes. It bridges conformal prediction and preference optimization, offering a principled approach for researchers in alignment and preference optimization. The framework handles both Bradley-Terry (BT) pairwise feedback and Plackett-Luce (PL) ranking feedback, and establishes finite-sample upper bounds on regularized policy suboptimality that separate approximation error, conformal uncertainty, and statistical estimation error. For BT feedback, a worst-case lower bound shows that uncertainty sets can induce a bias that persists as the optimization sample grows.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a method for aligning language models with human preferences by directly optimizing a policy from pairwise preference data, without needing a separate reward model. Conformal prediction is a distribution-free uncertainty quantification technique that produces prediction sets with guaranteed coverage under the assumption of exchangeability. The Bradley-Terry model is a standard probabilistic model for pairwise comparisons, often used to model human preferences.

## [StateWeaver: Multi-Agent Framework for Consistent Long-Horizon Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34290) ⭐️ 7.0/10

StateWeaver introduces a multi-agent coordination framework that combines Active States and Hierarchical Collaboration to maintain visual consistency and correctly depict state transitions in long-horizon video generation. The framework translates state discrepancies identified during review into concrete visual asset requirements, and links confirmed generation results to asset states, story time, and reference versions for reuse by subsequent shots. This work addresses a known hard problem in video foundation models—maintaining consistency over long videos—by using an agentic workflow that could influence how future video generation systems plan and repair content across shots. It may enable more reliable long-form video creation for applications like storytelling and film production. StateWeaver isolates execution contexts by task, clip, and repair round using hierarchical memory, and outperforms existing methods across multiple benchmarks under both predefined and autonomously planned storyboards. The approach preserves identity and already-correct content while repairing target attributes, though the abstract does not specify quantitative improvements or computational costs.

## [WebVibe: Reverse Synthesis and Self-Distillation for Interactive Website Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34292) ⭐️ 7.0/10

WebVibe introduces a reverse synthesis pipeline that verifies cross-page and in-page interactions in the browser before deriving multimodal inputs, building the 31,542-site WebVibe-32K corpus with reusable execution contracts. It also presents Interactive-SDPO, which conditions a weight-sharing self-teacher on replay traces and a 2x2 state-transition montage to provide token-level supervision at zero inference-time overhead, training InteractiveCoder models that achieve 55.2 functional score on Vision2Web L2 (nearly 3x the base model) and outperform baselines by over 23 points on WebVibeBench. This work addresses two key bottlenecks in training multimodal LLMs for interactive website generation: noisy supervision from forward synthesis and loss of state-transition information in scalar-reward reinforcement learning. The approach enables a 9B-parameter model to match a 1T-parameter coding agent in a single pass with 111x fewer parameters and 78x fewer tokens, suggesting a more efficient path to capable web-generation agents. The reverse synthesis flips the usual X -> Y (specification to code) pipeline into Y -> X by executing and verifying interactions first, so that unimplemented or altered requirements do not become supervision noise. Interactive-SDPO uses a weight-sharing self-teacher conditioned on replay traces and a 2x2 state-transition montage, providing token-level supervision without extra inference cost; the 9B model reaches 55.2 FS on Vision2Web L2 versus 20.2 for its base model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models can turn a single screenshot into static HTML, but generating fully interactive, multi-page websites from design specifications remains difficult due to routing and stateful widget transitions. Forward data synthesis writes the specification before code execution, so requirements that are not implemented or get altered remain in the specification as noise. Reinforcement learning with scalar rewards also discards the rich state transitions observed during replay. Self-distillation (SDPO) is a related technique that uses rich feedback beyond scalar rewards to improve credit assignment.
