## [FEAST: Task-Free Adaptive Sampling for On-Policy Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33827) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper identifies a feedback loop in on-policy learning where under-trained capabilities receive fewer opportunities, formalizes it via the Quota-Efficiency Law (QEL), and proposes FEAST, a task-free adaptive sampling framework that separates opportunity allocation from compute allocation. FEAST dynamically discovers capability clusters, preserves their learning histories, and reserves rollout budgets for each cluster, progressively concentrating computation on prompts with informative reward variation. This work addresses a fundamental inefficiency in training language agents from self-generated experience, where naive prioritization of strong learning signals can starve weaker capabilities. By ensuring continued sampling opportunities for all capability clusters, FEAST could lead to more balanced and robust agent learning, impacting how practitioners allocate limited rollout budgets in reinforcement learning. FEAST is evaluated on GURU-18K and SMC benchmarks, showing improved average performance over competitive adaptive sampling baselines under matched rollout budgets, while better preserving learning progress on weaker capabilities. Ablation studies confirm the complementary roles of cross-cluster opportunity protection and within-cluster selective allocation, and code will be released upon acceptance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: On-policy learning is a reinforcement learning paradigm where an agent learns from experiences generated by its own current policy, rather than from a fixed dataset. In this setting, allocating a limited rollout budget across heterogeneous capabilities is challenging because past allocation decisions change the policy and thus the evidence available for future decisions, potentially creating a feedback loop that neglects under-trained capabilities. Adaptive sampling methods aim to focus computation on the most informative experiences, but often prioritize capabilities with strong current learning signals, which can exacerbate this imbalance.

**Tags**: `#reinforcement-learning`, `#language-agents`, `#adaptive-sampling`, `#on-policy-learning`, `#curriculum-learning`

<a id="item-325"></a>
## [CAFE: Correlation-Aware Evidential Fusion for Trusted Multi-View Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33900) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes CAFE (Correlation-Aware Fusion of Evidence), a framework that incorporates inter-view correlations into evidential fusion for trusted multi-view classification. It applies complementarity-based trust discounting and a correlation-aware constraint to reduce redundant evidence from highly correlated views, then learns sample-specific weights to combine correlation-adjusted private evidence with cross-view collaborative evidence. Existing evidential multi-view methods treat views as independent sources, which can overcount consistent information and yield miscalibrated uncertainty. CAFE addresses this limitation and shows consistent gains in accuracy and calibration on six real-world datasets, with particularly strong improvements when views are highly correlated. CAFE introduces complementarity-based trust discounting for view-private experts and a correlation-aware constraint to penalize repeated contribution of similar evidence. It then adaptively combines correlation-adjusted private evidence with cross-view collaborative evidence using sample-specific weights learned from the global multi-view context.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Trusted multi-view classification aims to integrate information from multiple views (e.g., different sensors or feature sets) to make accurate predictions while also estimating uncertainty. Many methods use Dempster-Shafer theory of evidence, where each view produces class evidence and uncertainty, and these are combined via aggregation rules. However, treating views as independent ignores correlations, which can lead to overconfident and miscalibrated results.

## [360UAV-ETrack: Panoramic Benchmark and VLA Framework for UAV Tracking Without Heading Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33934) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces 360UAV-ETrack, a panoramic simulation benchmark for embodied UAV tracking without heading adjustment, comprising 2,652 sequences, over 1.73 million synchronized observations, eight tracking tasks, and 150 held-out closed-loop scenarios. It also proposes 360UAV-TVLA, a vision-language-action framework that improves normalized average tracking frames from 22.56% to 35.28% for the π0 backbone and from 25.68% to 33.64% for π0.5 compared to standard fine-tuning. This work decouples target observability from heading control by leveraging full-spherical perception, potentially enabling simpler and more robust UAV tracking in complex environments. The benchmark and framework provide a challenging testbed that highlights the gap between offline imitation accuracy and closed-loop reliability, which could spur further research in embodied AI for aerial robotics. The 360UAV-TVLA framework integrates persistent visual referring (PVR), compressed temporal memory (CTM), and geometry-aligned trajectory supervision (GTS) for instance identification, motion reasoning, and three-dimensional control. Despite improvements, the normalized average tracking frames remain at only about 35%, indicating that closed-loop tracking reliability is still far from solved.

**Background**: Conventional embodied UAV tracking relies on forward-facing cameras, so the target can leave the field of view, coupling observability with heading control. Vision-language-action (VLA) models are neural networks that combine visual perception, language understanding, and action generation, often built upon large pretrained models like π0 and π0.5.

## [DuoMI: Dual Mutual Information Regularization for Expert Reuse in Looped Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33952) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DuoMI introduces dual mutual information regularization to encourage expert reuse across rounds in looped mixture-of-experts Transformers without adding parameters. On a looped OLMoE backbone, it improves the average score across 15 downstream tasks from 50.08% to 51.82%, a gain of 1.74 percentage points over the looped baseline. This work addresses a key inefficiency in looped MoE models where routers may select different experts across rounds, limiting parameter reuse. The method adds no parameters and maintains sparse Top-k routing at inference, making it a practical technique for improving looped MoE architectures that are gaining traction for efficient scaling. DuoMI uses round-level marginal mutual information to limit dependence of aggregate expert usage on the round, and token-level conditional mutual information to penalize average cross-round routing changes outside a target interval. It computes differentiable penalties from soft routing distributions during training, adds no model parameters, and leaves sparse Top-k routing unchanged at inference.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-experts (MoE) is a technique where multiple expert networks specialize in different regions of the input space, with a router selecting which experts to activate per token. Looped Transformers reuse the same layers or experts across multiple rounds of computation to increase effective depth without adding parameters. In looped MoE models, routers across rounds may favor different expert subsets, reducing the benefit of sharing experts. Mutual information regularization is a method to control statistical dependence between variables, often used to encourage independence or invariance.

## [RefMix: Expanding Endpoint Proposals for Diversity-Preserving Reward Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33957) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RefMix introduces a reference-assisted endpoint proposal strategy for online forward matching reward fine-tuning of flow-based generative models. It injects alternative candidates from a reference model during early high-noise steps to preserve visual diversity, achieving up to 44% higher diversity over baselines at comparable reward levels. This work addresses the diversity loss and mode collapse that plague online reward fine-tuning in generative models, offering a practical solution that maintains task reward performance. It could benefit researchers and practitioners applying reward fine-tuning to flow-based models for image generation, where maintaining diverse outputs is crucial. RefMix uses a reference model during early high-noise steps to expose alternative visual modes, followed by the current reward-tuned model for endpoint completion. Controlled inference experiments show that training-time endpoint exposure is critical for improving the reward-diversity trade-off, beyond the impact of reference-assisted inference alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Flow-based generative models, such as those using flow matching, generate data by learning a continuous transformation from noise to data. Online forward matching reward fine-tuning repeatedly generates endpoints, rewards them, and fits the model to high-reward endpoints, but this on-policy feedback loop can reduce diversity as high-reward modes dominate. Mode collapse, where models produce less diverse outputs, is a known issue in generative models, often exacerbated by preference-learning approaches like RLHF.

## [Embodied-VGGT improves long-sequence 3D reconstruction with memory-augmented chunks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33964) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Embodied-VGGT introduces Memory-Augmented Grounded Chunks (MAGC), Superpoint Alignment (SA), and Cross-Loop Optimization (CL) to correct drift while preserving reliable local geometry in long-sequence embodied reconstruction. On KITTI Odometry, it reduces mean absolute trajectory error to 7.30 m (46.8% lower) and Chamfer distance to 2.67 m (23.3% lower) compared to the state-of-the-art baseline. This work addresses a key challenge in robotics and autonomous driving: maintaining accurate 3D reconstruction over long sequences where drift accumulates. The significant error reductions on public driving benchmarks could enable more reliable mapping and localization for autonomous systems. MAGC builds on Scal3R's global context memory and uses relative scale references from predicted ground geometry to regularize memory updates. SA balances ground and non-ground evidence for inter-chunk translation estimation, while CL selects coherent chunk groups for shared Sim(3) correction during global optimization. Code is released for reproducibility.

**Background**: Long-sequence 3D reconstruction from visual data is essential for robotics and autonomous driving but suffers from accumulated drift over time. VGGT (Visual Geometry Grounded Transformer) is a feed-forward neural network that predicts 3D attributes like camera parameters and depth maps from multiple views. Scal3R extends VGGT with a global context memory to handle large-scale reconstruction by retaining information across chunks. Embodied-VGGT builds on these ideas to improve consistency in embodied scenarios.

Aegis-VLA introduces a world-model-guided vision-language-action framework that predicts action corrections and uses a reliability gate to decide when to apply them for dynamic object manipulation. The Trajectory World Model combines kinematic extrapolation with learned residual dynamics to predict object motion and estimate positional uncertainty. Discrepancy-Guided Action Refinement proposes bounded corrections based on differences between history-only and base-action-conditioned forecasts, while the Reliability Gate regulates application using predictive uncertainty and anticipated object-end-effector proximity; rejected corrections fall back to base actions.

A new arXiv paper analyzes 51,240 stories generated by five models from 10,248 common prompts in the StoryScope corpus, introducing a Residual Convergence Index (RCI) and a prompt-feature semantic relevance mask. A new arXiv paper evaluates machine learning ensembles against published admission quotas using a 2022–2026 Vietnamese undergraduate panel of 26,895 program-year records from 309 universities. This work matters for ministries and institutional analysts who rely on published quotas as default intake projections, showing that naive log-growth ensembles can degrade accuracy and that admissions reforms can break conformal coverage guarantees. ## [MUSE: Unified Self-Supervised Transformer for mmWave Human Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34122) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MUSE introduces a multi-task uncertainty-aware self-supervised transformer encoder that unifies five mmWave human sensing datasets, replacing separate task-specific models with a single shared backbone. It achieves a 9.7 percentage point improvement in classification performance while reducing backbone parameters by N times. This work addresses the scalability and reliability challenges of mmWave ISAC models by enabling joint learning across tasks and providing uncertainty estimates for selective prediction. It could impact 6G ISAC deployments where multiple sensing tasks must run efficiently on a single system. MUSE uses per-dataset tokenization and masked autoencoding for pre-training, and evidential classification heads for uncertainty quantification during fine-tuning. It further improves confidence reliability with temperature scaling and conformal prediction, and shows uncertainty remains reliable under cross-user deployment, recovering 9.2 percentage points of accuracy through selective prediction without retraining.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) allows a wireless system to use the same waveform for both communication and environment sensing, and at mmWave bands it enables high-precision human sensing tasks like localization, gesture recognition, and pose estimation. Traditionally, each task requires a dedicated model trained from scratch on task-specific data, which limits scalability and transferability. Self-supervised learning, particularly masked autoencoding with transformers, has emerged as a way to pre-train on large unlabeled datasets and fine-tune for multiple downstream tasks. Uncertainty quantification helps flag unreliable predictions, and techniques like temperature scaling and conformal prediction calibrate confidence estimates.

## [Multi-view fNIRS reveals three neurophysiological subtypes in post-stroke aphasia](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34169) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study applied similarity network fusion and spectral clustering to three fNIRS feature views from 79 post-stroke aphasia patients, identifying three stable neurophysiological subtypes (n=32, 17, 30) that were significant after permutation testing (adjusted P=0.001) and stable under subsampling (mean adjusted Rand index 0.773). This work suggests that multi-view fNIRS analysis can uncover neurophysiological subgroups that are not captured by conventional aphasia classifications, potentially leading to more personalized rehabilitation strategies and better understanding of recovery mechanisms. The clusters were distinguished mainly by intrinsic activity and resting-state connectivity, with few differences in task activation; cluster membership was not significantly associated with conventional aphasia subtype (Cramér's V=0.287) or baseline clinical scores, and agreement dropped when the resting-state view was removed (adjusted Rand index 0.217), highlighting its importance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Post-stroke aphasia is a language disorder caused by brain damage from stroke, and patients show heterogeneous symptoms and recovery patterns. Functional near-infrared spectroscopy (fNIRS) is a non-invasive neuroimaging technique that measures brain activity by monitoring blood oxygenation changes. Similarity network fusion integrates multiple data views into a single similarity network, and spectral clustering groups data points using the eigenvalues of that network's similarity matrix.

## [Hierarchical Semantic Learning Framework Handles Missing Modalities in Multimodal Sentiment Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34184) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Hierarchical Semantic Learning (HSL), a framework that decouples each modality encoder's output into low-, mid-, and high-level semantic streams and uses cross-modal cross-level reconstruction to recover missing modalities. It also applies prototypical contrastive regularization to stabilize reconstruction and hierarchical interactive fusion for robust predictions, outperforming baselines on CMU-MOSI, CMU-MOSEI, IEMOCAP, and CH-SIMS in most missing-modality settings. Real-world multimodal sentiment analysis systems often face unpredictable missing modalities due to sensor failures or privacy masking, and HSL's hierarchical approach addresses this by leveraging deep encoder semantics rather than flattened representations. This could improve robustness in affective computing applications where data streams are unreliable. HSL's reconstruction uses high-level semantics from observed modalities as global anchors to progressively recover mid-level structures and low-level details of missing modalities, while a hierarchical cascade error supervisor with prototypical contrastive regularization enforces level-specific semantic consistency. Experiments show improvements in most missing-modality settings, though the abstract does not specify the exact margin or computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Multimodal sentiment analysis combines text, audio, and visual signals to predict sentiment, but in practice some modalities may be missing. Existing methods either learn reliability-aware representations or reconstruct missing inputs, often from a single flattened encoder output, which overlooks the semantic hierarchy of deep encoders and causes semantic averaging and ill-posed mappings. Hierarchical learning, inspired by the layered feature extraction in deep networks like CNNs, aims to exploit multiple levels of abstraction for more robust representations.

GeoCAM introduces a self-supervised framework that learns a shared embedding space for side-scan sonar tiles and underwater optical images without semantic annotations, using a Set Attention Aggregator, a geometry-weighted InfoNCE loss based on GPS-derived footprint overlap, and an optional cross-modal masked reconstruction branch. Experiments on the BenthiCat SSS-CAM dataset show improved cross-modal retrieval and alignment over strong contrastive baselines. This work addresses the underexplored problem of cross-modal self-supervised learning for paired sonar and optical data, which is crucial for benthic habitat understanding and marine robotics. By leveraging geographic metadata and one-to-many correspondences, it enables learning from abundant unlabeled marine survey data, potentially improving automated seafloor mapping and monitoring. The method converts GPS-derived footprint overlap into soft positive supervision for a geometry-weighted InfoNCE objective, and the Set Attention Aggregator handles multiple optical observations per sonar tile. The approach is evaluated on the BenthiCat SSS-CAM dataset, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Side-scan sonar is an acoustic imaging system that produces high-resolution 2D images of the seafloor, even in murky water, and is widely used in marine surveys. Cross-modal learning between sonar and optical imagery is challenging because the two modalities have different physical properties and annotations are scarce. Self-supervised contrastive learning, such as InfoNCE, learns representations by pulling positive pairs together and pushing negatives apart, but requires defining what constitutes a positive pair. GeoCAM uses geographic overlap from GPS to define soft positives, and employs a set attention aggregator to handle multiple optical views per sonar tile.

The approach could help improve segmentation accuracy in data-constrained clinical settings, potentially benefiting prostate cancer diagnosis and treatment planning. The diffusion model conditions on stacks of adjacent binary masks via channel concatenation and generates volumes consistent with the conditioning anatomy while diversifying tissue appearance. On the LUND probe cohort (n=432), a 23% synthetic mix reduced mean false-positive voxels from 11,102 to 10,757, a modest improvement, while the MSD cohort (n=32) showed a more substantial reduction from 4,201 to 3,229.

## [ConTra: RL-trained prefix generators for contextual retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34366) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ConTra is a reinforcement learning framework that trains lightweight prefix generators to preserve document context in chunked text for improved retrieval and reranking. Its 2B model outperforms a prompted 9B baseline and a 4B contextual encoder by up to 3.15 points while indexing short documents 7.74x and 2.15x faster, respectively. This approach addresses a key indexing challenge in RAG systems: keeping chunks independently searchable while retaining context, and it does so with a small model that is cheaper and faster than large prompted baselines. It could make contextual retrieval more practical for large-scale document collections. ConTra generates one prefix per chunk during index construction and stores the augmented text and its embedding for reuse across queries. The reward combines feedback from frozen embedding and reranking models with factual verification and length control, and the authors introduce RealContextBench with five application collections and three controlled conditions to evaluate robustness to distracting text.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems split long documents into smaller chunks for indexing, but chunking can strip away identifying details and context. Contextual retrieval methods address this by adding context to each chunk, either by encoding it in vectors or by attaching a text prefix, but vector-only context is invisible to text rerankers and prompted prefixes are costly to generate. ConTra uses reinforcement learning to train a small prefix generator that produces context-aware prefixes efficiently.

This arXiv paper compares different calibration modules within a single differentiable upper limb neuromusculoskeletal model to identify an optimal pipeline for improving model accuracy. The study evaluates 16 calibration configurations across 9 testing movements and 4 subjects, finding that muscle synergy extrapolation reduces torque loss by 23.6% on average and bone dynamic parameter optimization reduces it by 10.9% on average. ProxyGenBench is a new benchmark introduced in arXiv paper 2611.34457 to evaluate video generators' ability to preserve structure, complete missing content, and bind multimodal conditions when guided by structural proxies such as animated 3D blockouts. It includes 312 evaluation cases from 60 scenes across three scene tasks, with controlled variations in proxy fidelity, text detail, and multimodal inputs, and evaluates five video generators using case-specific checklists. This benchmark addresses a growing subfield of controllable video generation, where structural proxies are used to direct scene layout, motion, and camera behavior. It provides a structured evaluation methodology that could help researchers identify trade-offs between proxy detail and content completion, and between structural adherence and correct multimodal binding, potentially guiding future model development. The benchmark reveals that while proxies improve spatiotemporal control, greater proxy detail can coincide with poorer completion, and strong structural adherence does not ensure correct binding of text, image, and audio conditions to the right entities and events. The evaluation uses verified, case-specific checklists and controlled variations to isolate these interacting requirements.

PURA introduces a training-free video token pruning method that unifies spatial and temporal redundancy assessment by constructing reference candidates for each token and selecting the one with the lowest representation error. It also proposes Coordinated Pruning, which batches compatible low-cost token removals to avoid sequential overhead, and demonstrates favorable performance-efficiency trade-offs on three video LLMs and five benchmarks. Video large language models face high inference costs due to long visual token sequences, and PURA's adaptive spatiotemporal assessment could enable more efficient deployment without retraining. This is particularly relevant for real-time video understanding applications where computational resources are limited. PURA is training-free and dynamically balances spatial and temporal compression per token, unlike methods with predefined orders or fixed ratios. The Coordinated Pruning batches mutually compatible low-cost removals and updates pruning state iteratively until the target budget is met, reducing time overhead.

**Background**: Video large language models process video frames into sequences of visual tokens, which can be very long and lead to high inference costs. Token pruning reduces these costs by removing redundant tokens, often leveraging spatial redundancy within frames and temporal redundancy across frames. Many existing methods use fixed allocation ratios or predefined processing orders, which may not adapt well to varying redundancy across different videos.

## [HAS: Hierarchical Adaptive Frame Sampling for Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34544) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Hierarchical Adaptive Frame Sampling (HAS), a two-stage framework that first uses a Determinantal Point Process (DPP) to select query-relevant and non-redundant backbone frames, then adaptively allocates the remaining frame budget between local and global context based on the temporal distribution of those frames. When integrated into three leading multimodal LLMs, HAS consistently outperforms baselines on the Video-MME, LongVideoBench, and MLVU benchmarks. Existing frame sampling methods for video understanding with multimodal LLMs are typically driven by a single principle, which fails to accommodate heterogeneous query requirements such as holistic temporal understanding versus fine-grained event analysis. HAS addresses this limitation and could improve efficiency and accuracy in video-based AI applications without requiring model retraining. The two-stage design uses DPP in the first stage to ensure diversity and relevance among selected frames, and in the second stage infers query requirements from the temporal distribution of backbone frames to allocate the remaining budget between Local Context (fine-grained dynamics and short-range causality) and Global Context (holistic view and long-range evidence). The method is training-free and model-agnostic, as demonstrated by integration with three leading MLLMs.

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) process video by sampling a subset of frames due to context-length constraints, but how to select frames is critical for understanding. Determinantal Point Processes (DPPs) are stochastic point processes that model negative correlations and are used in machine learning for efficient diverse sampling. Video understanding benchmarks like Video-MME, LongVideoBench, and MLVU evaluate models on long and complex videos, where adaptive frame selection can significantly impact performance.

## [Information-Theoretic Framework for Hiding Data in Semantic Communication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34550) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper establishes an information-theoretic framework for information hiding in semantic communication, extending the classical Moulin-O'Sullivan hiding model to a two-layer structure with four distortion constraints. It formulates the hiding capacity as a Stackelberg game with a single-letter maximin expression and shows that semantic hiding is not unconditionally superior to classical hiding. This work bridges two active research areas—semantic communication and information hiding—providing a theoretical foundation for understanding the limits and trade-offs of hiding information at the semantic layer. It could inform the design of secure and efficient semantic communication systems, though its immediate practical impact is likely limited to theoretical research. The framework introduces synonymous mappings f_s, f_x, and f_y^{-1} to bridge syntactic and semantic layers, and imposes syntactic transparency, semantic fidelity, syntactic perturbation, and semantic destruction constraints. The capacity is characterized via a Stackelberg game where the hider moves first, and comparisons show that closing semantic tolerance yields a capacity no greater than the classical capacity under fair comparison.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Semantic communication is an emerging paradigm that focuses on transmitting the meaning of data rather than raw bits, potentially improving efficiency. Information hiding is the practice of embedding secret data within a host signal, classically analyzed using information-theoretic models. The Stackelberg game is a strategic model where a leader moves first and followers respond, often used to model adversarial or sequential decision-making scenarios.

## [Generalized Graph Learning Predicts Hurricane Evacuation Traffic Across Multiple Storms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34687) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose a generalized dynamic graph learning framework that uses traffic detector and evacuation data from 12 historical hurricanes to predict evacuation traffic up to 6 hours in advance. For an unseen hurricane (Milton, 2024), the model achieves an RMSE of 280.6 and R² of 0.96 for 1-hour prediction, and an RMSE of 459.8 and R² of 0.89 for 6-hour prediction. Accurate evacuation traffic prediction can help traffic management officials take proactive measures to reduce congestion, delays, and safety risks during hurricanes. The model's strong generalization to an unseen hurricane suggests it could be deployed across different storms without retraining, improving disaster response efficiency. The model uses a Graph Convolutional Long Short-Term Memory (GCN-LSTM) architecture and a modular training approach that sequentially incorporates data from multiple hurricanes. Ablation experiments show an 8.4% improvement in RMSE for the dynamic GCN-LSTM compared to its static counterpart, and the model outperforms state-of-the-art baselines for overall 6-hour prediction horizons.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) are deep learning models designed to work with graph-structured data, such as transportation networks where nodes are detectors and edges represent road connections. Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) is a type of recurrent neural network good at capturing temporal dependencies. Combining GCN and LSTM allows modeling both spatial and temporal patterns in traffic data, which is essential for predicting dynamic evacuation traffic across different hurricanes.

<a id="item-353"></a>
## [Mismatch-Corrected Bandits Improve Proxy-Based Data Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34707) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes mismatch-corrected bandits that model the ranking discrepancy between a small proxy model and a larger target model using paired audits, and incorporates this correction into data allocation decisions. The method casts the problem as a multi-fidelity bandit and proves regret bounds showing that proxy-only allocation can incur linear regret, while a sliding-window policy achieves regret of order √(Γ_T T log T). This work addresses a fundamental inefficiency in proxy-based data selection for training large models: the proxy's ranking may not align with the target's, leading to suboptimal training. The theoretical guarantees and empirical gains (6.3–10.5 percentage points improvement in worst-group accuracy) suggest that correcting for proxy-target mismatch can make data-efficient training more reliable, which is relevant for practitioners using small models to guide large model training. The method uses paired observations from rare audits to identify arm-wise mismatches without assuming a shared offset or preserved rankings, and it audits only uncertain decisions while pacing the audit budget over training. Experiments on CivilComments-WILDS with BERT-Small guiding BERT-Large show that spreading audits over training improves worst-group accuracy by 6.3 to 10.5 percentage points at the same audit count compared to using the budget early.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: In data-efficient machine learning, a common strategy is to use a small proxy model to score and select training data for a larger target model, under the assumption that the proxy's rankings transfer. However, the proxy and target may rank data differently, causing a mismatch that degrades target performance. Multi-fidelity bandits are a framework for sequential decision-making where rewards can be observed at different fidelities (e.g., cheap proxy vs. expensive target), and regret measures the performance loss relative to an oracle. This paper combines these ideas to correct for proxy-target mismatch in data sampling.

**Tags**: `#bandits`, `#data-sampling`, `#multi-fidelity`, `#machine-learning`, `#arxiv`

## [Study introduces 'answerability' metric for scene-graph memory in embodied QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34745) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new exploratory study defines 'answerability' as the fraction of questions a fixed rule-based reader answers correctly from a vision-language scene graph alone, and reports that four targeted repairs doubled object recall and raised answerability from 27.2% to 37.4% while improving retrained QA accuracy from 71.4% to 75.9%. However, the study also finds that better object detection F1 can lower answerability, and that a question-type prior outperforms the graph-based reader on every predicted graph. This work suggests that answer accuracy alone conflates retained evidence, reading errors, and question priors, so scoring retained evidence via answerability could lead to more reliable evaluation of memory systems in embodied question answering. It also warns that improvements in perception metrics like object detection do not necessarily translate to better downstream question answering. The baseline pipeline recovers only 24.1% of visible object types and its room labels flicker, and in one real-video test, graph memory actually lowers accuracy, highlighting limitations of the simulated multi-room setting. The findings favor scoring retained evidence alongside accuracy rather than relying solely on end-task performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Embodied question answering (EQA) requires an agent to explore an environment and answer questions about it, often using a scene graph as memory—a structured representation of objects and their relationships. Answer accuracy is the standard metric, but it can be misleading because it mixes what the agent actually retained with errors in reading the graph and biases from the types of questions asked. This study isolates the memory component by measuring how many questions a simple rule-based reader can answer correctly from the graph alone.

## [SEPO: Evolving System Prompt Ensembles for Creative LLM Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34760) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduce SEPO, a training-free framework that evolves an ensemble of behaviorally distinct system prompts and samples one prompt per response at inference time. Across four creative generation tasks and three open-weight models, SEPO ranks first in 8 of 12 settings on the harmonic mean of quality, diversity, and novelty. The work addresses mode collapse in preference-aligned LLMs, where outputs cluster around high-reward responses and stifle creativity. By improving diversity and novelty at inference time without retraining, it offers a practical approach for practitioners seeking more original LLM outputs. SEPO combines prompt evolution with greedy max-min selection under a quality constraint, then uniformly samples one prompt per response. On average, it raises LLM-judged diversity from 1.92 to 3.43 and novelty from 1.00 to 3.34 (1-5 scale) over base sampling, while quality decreases from 3.72 to 3.30.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Preference-aligned LLMs are often fine-tuned to produce high-reward responses, which can cause mode collapse: the model repeatedly generates similar, safe outputs. Creative generation requires balancing quality, novelty, and diversity, and existing test-time methods often prioritize diversity without ensuring each response is both high-quality and novel. System prompt ensembles involve using multiple distinct prompts to steer model behavior, and SEPO evolves such an ensemble to cover diverse behaviors.

## [InterConRank: Joint Node Centrality in Interconnected Heterogeneous Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34763) ⭐️ 6.0/10

InterConRank is a new centrality algorithm that computes separate node rankings for each layer of an interconnected network, with directed dependencies specifying which layers supply scores to each receiving layer. It normalizes each link type separately and updates all layers together, and the authors prove convergence of this joint update and show on four scholarly networks that it achieves the highest rank correlations with h-index and total citations for nodes in the collaboration layer. This work addresses a gap in network analysis: traditional centrality measures ignore cross-layer dependencies, so two nodes with identical intra-layer connections can be ranked differently when their neighbors in other layers occupy different positions. The algorithm could improve author impact assessment in scholarly networks and be applied to other interconnected systems such as transportation or biological networks. The algorithm allows a score to cross into a layer in one iteration and propagate along its internal links in the next, and convergence is proven for the joint update. Empirical validation is limited to four scholarly collaboration networks, and the abstract does not report comparisons with other multilayer centrality methods or computational complexity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Centrality measures such as degree, betweenness, and PageRank identify important nodes in a graph, but most assume a single-layer network. Interconnected heterogeneous networks consist of multiple layers (e.g., collaboration, citation, and co-authorship layers) where nodes are linked by different types of relationships, and a node's importance may depend on its position across all layers. The h-index is an author-level metric that combines productivity and citation impact, and total citations is a simple sum of citations received.

## [HOG-WAM boosts visual robustness of world-action models via future gradient prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34811) ⭐️ 6.0/10

HOG-WAM introduces a training-only auxiliary HOG diffusion transformer that supervises the visual representation of FastWAM with future Histograms of Oriented Gradients (HOG) derived from demonstration images. This improves success rates under observation corruptions, e.g., mixed-noise success on RoboTwin-Plus rises from 78.86% to 82.86% and LIBERO Plus overall success from 74.49% to 75.26%. This work shows that adding structural supervision from HOG can make world-action models more robust to visual corruption without changing the inference architecture, which is valuable for real-world robot deployment where sensor noise and occlusions are common. The larger absolute gains on physical Franka tasks (40%→59% clean, 17%→24% noisy) suggest practical benefits beyond simulation. The HOG diffusion transformer module reads RGB features through asymmetric attention and is removed at inference, preserving FastWAM's RGB-to-action architecture. An ablation favors combining RGB observations with auxiliary HOG prediction over replacing RGB with HOG, and the code is available at https://github.com/iPPLab/HOG-WAM.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: World-action models (WAMs) jointly predict future observations and actions, often by fine-tuning pretrained video backbones into robot policies. Histograms of Oriented Gradients (HOG) is a classical feature descriptor that captures edge orientation distributions to describe object shape and appearance. Diffusion transformers (DiTs) are generative models that use transformer architectures to denoise data, and here an auxiliary DiT predicts future HOG features as a training signal.

## [DRBench Pro: A Knowledge-Centric Benchmark for Deep Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34852) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DRBench Pro is a new benchmark that evaluates deep research agents by measuring structured knowledge coverage, using knowledge trees derived from Wikipedia to assess breadth and depth. It includes 144 cases across 24 domains and 44,102 rubrics, and evaluating 16 agents shows the best-performing agent reaches 57.04% overall coverage. This benchmark addresses a gap in existing evaluations by focusing on which research directions a report covers and how deeply it explores them, providing a more structured and knowledge-centric assessment. It could help standardize evaluation of deep research agents and drive improvements in their coverage of complex topics. The automated pipeline builds local knowledge trees from Wikipedia and uses sampled subgraphs to generate research queries and evidence-grounded rubrics, with each rubric corresponding to a relation in the tree. Rubric judgments agree closely with human annotations, and agent rankings remain stable across tested metric configurations.

**Background**: Deep research agents are AI systems that gather information from multiple sources and synthesize reports on complex, open-ended questions. Existing benchmarks assess report quality but lack a dedicated knowledge-centric evaluation of coverage breadth and depth. DRBench Pro introduces structured knowledge coverage as the primary evaluation target, using Wikipedia-derived knowledge trees to generate queries and rubrics.

## [Decision-Focused Fine-Tuning of Tabular Transformers for Cost-Sensitive Financial Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34858) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes fine-tuning autoregressive tabular transformers directly on a decision/expected-utility objective under institution-specified, amount-scaled cost matrices, instead of relying on post-hoc Bayes-optimal thresholding of calibrated probabilities for cost-sensitive financial event prediction. This approach aligns model training with actual business decisions, potentially improving outcomes in high-stakes domains like fraud detection where misclassification costs are asymmetric and vary with transaction amount. The method uses a closed-form expected-utility objective and gradient because the reward is a known function of action, outcome, and amount, and the outcome is observed; it also includes a KL anchor to the pretrained model and an auxiliary language-modeling term to preserve calibration and the generative event model, with a sampled GRPO-style variant as an extension point.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Autoregressive transformers trained on tokenized tabular event streams are strong baselines for predicting financial events like fraud. Typically, institutions convert predicted probabilities into actions using Bayes-optimal thresholding under a cost matrix, but this two-step approach may not optimize the final decision objective. Decision-focused learning instead trains models to directly maximize expected utility, and cost-sensitive learning accounts for asymmetric misclassification costs.

## [RePace: Offline Reasoning Adaptively Speeds Up Demonstrations for Faster VLA Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34864) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose RePace, an offline reasoning framework that adaptively determines per-segment speedup factors for human demonstrations used to train vision-language-action (VLA) policies. Unlike prior methods that assign predefined speedup factors to segments, RePace uses a pretrained language model to interpret candidate retiming effects from visual context and motion, selecting a speedup profile for the entire episode. VLA policies often inherit the slow pace of human demonstrations, and accelerating demonstrations before fine-tuning can yield faster policies without adding a speed-selection module at deployment. RePace addresses a key limitation of predefined speedup methods by adapting speed within segments, potentially improving task execution efficiency in robotic manipulation. RePace measures how much each candidate speedup factor shortens the sequence and compares the resulting observation-action pairs used for training. Experiments on LIBERO and RoboCasa-Kitchen show that RePace outperforms baselines in overall task execution efficiency, and on a Franka Research 3 arm it matches or exceeds acceleration baselines in success rate while completing successful trials faster.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that integrate visual perception, natural language instructions, and motor control to enable robots to perform tasks. They are often trained via imitation learning from human demonstrations, but the slow pace of demonstrations can lead to sluggish robot behavior. Offline speedup methods aim to accelerate demonstrations before fine-tuning, but typically assign fixed speedup factors to segments, which may be suboptimal when precision requirements vary within a segment.

## [Survey Maps Security, Privacy, and Ethics Risks of LLMs in Telecom](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34868) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv survey (2611.34868) examines the privacy, security, and ethical risks of deploying large language models in telecommunications, introducing a telecom-specific taxonomy that links these risks to concrete use cases such as customer service, network analytics, and operational support. It also outlines open research challenges including longitudinal data privacy, cross-border compliance, and scalable guardrails. As telecom operators increasingly integrate LLMs into customer-facing and network-facing systems, the unique sensitivity and longitudinal nature of telecom data raise risks that generic AI safety frameworks may not address. This survey provides practitioners with a structured taxonomy and highlights compliance and guardrail gaps that could shape future deployment strategies and regulatory discussions. The paper is a survey with no novel technical contributions or empirical results; its value lies in the proposed taxonomy and the identification of open challenges. The challenges of longitudinal data privacy, cross-border compliance, and scalable guardrails are highlighted as particularly pressing for telecom because data is collected over long periods and across jurisdictions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are AI systems trained on vast text corpora to understand and generate human-like language, and they are increasingly used in telecom for tasks like chatbots, network log analysis, and operational decision support. Telecom data is uniquely sensitive because it includes call detail records, location data, and usage patterns that can reveal personal behavior over time. Longitudinal data refers to repeated observations of the same subjects over long periods, which amplifies privacy risks. Cross-border compliance involves adhering to different data protection laws (e.g., GDPR) when data flows across jurisdictions, and guardrails are safety mechanisms that constrain LLM outputs to prevent harmful or non-compliant behavior.

## [Shape-Aware Three-Way Merge for RDF Knowledge Graphs Using SHACL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34870) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper presents a three-way merge for RDF knowledge graphs where SHACL shapes define conflicts, implemented in a production multi-agent store and evaluated against seven other strategies. In tests across five synthetic seeds, the shape-aware operator raised 33 conflicts (all true positives) with no triples lost or fabricated, while the context-overlap baseline detected the same conflicts but required 845 questions. This work reduces human decision-making in merging RDF knowledge graphs by using schema constraints to automatically detect conflicts, which could improve collaborative knowledge engineering workflows. It extends the Quit Store's Context Merge approach and provides empirical evidence for shape-aware conflict detection in production settings. The approach uses set algebra to combine canonical triples and only treats a slot as contended when a SHACL shape bounds its cardinality and both sides' changes cannot stand within that bound; unbounded predicates are unioned. A replay of production incidents revealed a triple-level limit: different names for one entity remain invisible (0 of 93 alias repairs detected), and under wrong shapes a missing bound loses data silently while a spurious one only adds questions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: RDF (Resource Description Framework) is a standard model for data interchange on the Web, representing information as triples of subject-predicate-object. Three-way merge is a version control technique that combines changes from two branches with their common ancestor, commonly used in software development. SHACL (Shapes Constraint Language) is a W3C standard for validating RDF graphs against a set of conditions, allowing schemas to define constraints like cardinality. The Quit Store is a distributed version control system for RDF datasets that uses Git-like operations and provides a Context Merge overlap rule for merging.

The paper introduces a hybrid decision tree architecture that integrates both feature-based and case-based splits, allowing heterogeneous splits throughout the tree. It also proposes a novel selective case-based split that searches for the most informative subset of features to define the split, balancing specificity and simplicity. This approach addresses the trade-off between interpretability and accuracy in decision trees, potentially offering a model that is both accurate and interpretable without relying on ensembles. It could benefit domains requiring transparent models, such as healthcare and finance. Experiments on diverse tabular datasets show the method outperforms standard decision trees and achieves performance on par with ensemble methods, while producing shallow trees that retain or improve predictive accuracy. The hybrid architecture allows heterogeneous splits, and the selective case-based split balances case specificity with feature simplicity.

A new arXiv paper (2611.34899) demonstrates that compressed latent-space embeddings from the TerraMind geospatial foundation model can detect, localize, and classify vessels in Sentinel-1 SAR imagery, achieving a ROC-AUC of 0.95 for detection. The authors also release a dataset of about 2,000 Sentinel-1 images paired with temporally aligned AIS signals, available on HuggingFace. It also provides a benchmark dataset that can spur further research in maritime domain awareness using geospatial foundation models. Vessel localization achieves a median error of around 300 m for vessels longer than 100 m, and AIS type-code classification reaches 62% accuracy for majority classes, though performance is highly correlated with vessel length. ## [ReviewSense: Cluster-Guided Evidence Selection for Review-to-Advice Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16466) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ReviewSense introduces a cluster-guided evidence-selection workflow that embeds reviews with a sentence transformer, groups them via iterative cosine-similarity clustering with threshold decay, and selects compact evidence before LLM generation. Evaluation on dental and healthcare reviews shows that five cluster representatives yield advice closest to expert recommendations while using far less context than fixed-generator baselines. This work positions evidence selection as the controlling design variable in review-to-advice generation, showing that how evidence is chosen matters more than the LLM itself. It offers a practical, inspectable human-in-the-loop pipeline that could improve decision support in healthcare and other service domains. A single central representative preserves the dominant cluster theme in 77.3% of cases but only 60.6% of secondary themes, indicating central evidence captures main issues more reliably than minority concerns. Five cluster representatives outperform a single representative as a front-end, and the clustering assigns every review while remaining competitive with standard algorithms across three domains.

**Background**: Customer reviews are a rich but weakly structured source of operational feedback, and turning them into actionable advice is challenging due to noise and volume. Sentence transformers convert text into dense embeddings that capture semantic meaning, enabling similarity-based grouping. Clustering then groups similar reviews, and representative evidence is selected to feed a large language model (LLM) for advice generation, reducing context length and focusing on key themes.

## [Agent Calibration Framework Treats Deployment Contracts as Versioned Acceptance Criteria](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35149) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.35149v4) defines 'agent calibration' as qualification against a versioned deployment contract that specifies basic capabilities, end-to-end reachability, technical and professional precision, and acceptance by intended users. For each requirement, a source-to-destination map records what must be preserved, transformed, added, or retired, and expert-supplied instruments guide changes to prompts, tool calls, or an external compact policy while the foundation model remains frozen. The framework addresses a practical reliability gap: agents can remain operational after a model, tool, or user-context change while silently losing capabilities required at their destination. By formalizing acceptance criteria and comparing adaptation costs (annotation, training, serving, review, rework), it gives teams a structured way to qualify agent revisions under deployment change rather than relying on aggregate scores. Three diagnostic audits illustrate the limits of aggregate scores: on a 100-item intent task each model solved cases the other missed, in a 60-record synthetic financial reporting fixture five records passed a numeric proxy but failed the reporting contract, and in a target-only WebMall snapshot quota interruptions affected 132 of 171 attempts. The paper motivates paired reruns, checkpoint replay, and separate measurement of repaired versus newly failed cases, and specifies controlled experiments to test whether calibration increases accepted deliveries at a given cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents are systems that combine a foundation model with prompts, tools, and orchestration code (often called a harness) to complete multi-step tasks. When the underlying model, tools, or user context change, an agent may keep running but lose capabilities that matter for its deployment target, a problem that aggregate benchmark scores can hide. This paper proposes treating deployment as a contract with explicit acceptance criteria and measuring the cost of maintaining the harness, including an optional external compact policy, while keeping the foundation model frozen.

## [FinAutoRubric: Expert-Guided Automatic Rubric Generation for Finance Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35744) ⭐️ 6.0/10

FinAutoRubric introduces a system in which experts supply reusable evaluation guidance while agents and code generate, review, and validate query-specific rubrics for evaluating financial research agents. On three expert-authored finance benchmarks, its rubrics track expert scoring as closely as the strongest evaluated generator while stating the expert rubric's expected value for more criteria, and in-house analysts preferred them in a blind review. Expert-reviewed finance benchmarks rely on fixed, per-item rubrics that are costly to extend and cannot encode each institution's own standards, so automatic rubric generation could make such evaluations far more scalable and customizable. This matters for teams building and auditing LLM-based financial research agents, where evaluation quality directly determines trust in agent outputs. Expert guidance is injected both as prompts to every agent and as rules enforced by code, with a Task Bank of reusable criteria carrying it across tasks; in long-horizon loops a writer agent researches each expected value and a reviewer agent verifies it, escalating failures to a human. The released 100-query FinAutoRubric Benchmark, drawn from in-house analysts' key questions across 78 tasks and eight asset classes, shows that rubrics from an earlier model generation still leave headroom for a later one.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Evaluating AI agents that perform financial research requires rubrics that reflect expert standards and pin down values correct as of a specific information cutoff, since financial facts change over time. Existing expert-reviewed benchmarks use fixed per-item rubrics, which are expensive to extend to new queries and hard to adapt to different institutions' standards. FinAutoRubric addresses this by combining reusable expert guidance with LLM agents that draft, check, and validate rubrics automatically.

## [ACTR Aligns Cross-Lingual Reasoning Traces for Safer Multilingual LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37054) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces ACTR (Aligning Cross-lingual Thoughts and Responses), a framework that improves multilingual safety alignment in reasoning LLMs by strengthening the use of existing safety reasoning. It proposes a think gap score (TGS) to measure how much reasoning traces contribute to attention outputs across languages, uses neuron masking to identify 'safety think neurons,' and applies neuron-selective consistency optimization (NSCO) with a frozen judge model to reward agreement between reasoning-trace and response safety categories. Across two reasoning models, ACTR achieves lower average attack success rates than state-of-the-art methods on AdvBench-X and MultiJail, with gains extending to unseen languages. Reasoning LLMs can identify safety risks in their chain-of-thought yet still produce unsafe responses when jailbroken in non-high-resource languages, a gap that undermines reliable global deployment. ACTR offers a training-time alignment method that requires no human-annotated responses or preference data, making multilingual safety improvement more scalable for developers of reasoning models. The method uses reasoning-trace substitution to measure the cross-lingual safety gap and compares high-importance neuron sets obtained with reasoning enabled versus disabled to isolate safety think neurons. NSCO updates only parameters associated with those selected neurons, and the approach preserves or improves average performance on multilingual knowledge and mathematical reasoning tasks while limiting false refusals of benign requests.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Jailbreak attacks are adversarial prompts designed to bypass an LLM's safety filters and alignment protocols, and they are especially effective in lower-resource languages where safety training data is scarce. Reasoning models generate intermediate chain-of-thought traces before answering, which can improve complex reasoning but do not guarantee that safety-relevant conclusions in the trace are reflected in the final response. Neuron-importance analysis estimates which internal parameters matter for a behavior by masking neurons and observing changes in model representations.

The paper introduces WoW (World Knowledge), a large-scale Chinese educational corpus built on an Educational Knowledge Graph that systematically collects and fills missing modules across all educational levels. This iteration adds multimodal signals, supports every stage of model development, and includes an interactive platform for graph-based data selection and retrieval. Experiments show that WoW significantly improves model performance across diverse benchmarks, and the dataset is designed to support full-cycle training from pre-training through later stages. ## [ε-WM: Error-Driven Fast-Slow Adaptation for Robot World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15215) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces ε-WM, a world model that adapts to sudden, unknown robot dynamics changes through error-driven corrections across fast and slow timescales. It incorporates multiple internal experts (MiE), fast-slow timescale adaptation, and predictive correction without gradients, achieving lower multi-step mean error than ten baselines on six datasets. This work addresses a critical challenge in robot world models—balancing responsiveness, stability, and persistence during open-loop rollout—which could improve robot control in dynamic environments. It offers a biologically inspired alternative to gradient-based adaptation, potentially enabling more robust and efficient real-time adaptation in robotics. ε-WM uses multiple internal experts, each combining a fixed base expert with fast-slow adaptation networks; fast states respond rapidly and decay, while slow states accumulate corrections and persist. Predictive correction updates states and reweights experts without gradients, and during open-loop rollout each expert feeds back its own predictions aggregated via adaptive weighting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: World models are learned simulators that predict future states and are used for planning and control in robotics. Adapting to sudden changes in robot dynamics (e.g., payload or friction) is difficult because models must react quickly without overfitting to transient noise and must retain adaptations during open-loop rollout when observations are unavailable. Predictive coding is a neuroscience theory suggesting the brain uses multi-timescale error signals to update internal models, inspiring the fast-slow adaptation mechanism in ε-WM.

## [KnowMe Paper Radar: Lightweight Discovery of Promising Papers in the Age of Autoresearch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23702) ⭐️ 6.0/10

KnowMe Paper Radar is a lightweight arXiv discovery tool that ranks papers by topical relevance weighted by author and institution reputation, using message passing to surface rising stars. In a sealed test on 10,386 new papers, those with an identity-bound author were twice as likely to reach the top tenth of early citations. This tool addresses the growing challenge of paper overload in the era of LLM-driven autoresearch, where citations arrive too late to identify impactful work. By leveraging author and institution reputation signals, it could help researchers and newcomers quickly find high-quality papers, potentially improving literature review and research efficiency. The tool uses message passing along collaborations to diffuse author credit and surface rising stars, and profiles institutions by the researchers they hold in each direction rather than by headcount. It is designed to grow into a scholarly knowledge graph queryable via a command-line interface (CLI) for future autoresearch and recursive self-improvement (RSI).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: arXiv is a popular open-access preprint server where researchers share papers before peer review, leading to a massive volume of new submissions daily. Author and institution reputation systems aim to assess the credibility of research based on the track record of the people and organizations involved, as established researchers tend to produce higher-quality work. Message passing is a technique used in network analysis to propagate information across nodes, here applied to diffuse credit through collaboration networks.

## [Study finds SFT can learn mixed reasoning modes, bottleneck shifts to local state tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30281) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2611.30281v2) formalizes the representational compatibility of autoregressive models with latent reasoning mixtures and shows through controlled experiments that standard supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on unlabeled mixed trajectories (BFS and DFS) performs comparably to homogeneous or labeled baselines under matched token budgets. Scaling from 0.6B to 14B parameters largely eliminates global mode confusion, shifting the primary bottleneck to local state-tracking transitions, where only a small fraction of errors are outcome-determining 'Critical Reasoning Nodes' (CRNs) that can be repaired with single-step fixes. This work suggests that practitioners can pool diverse reasoning trajectories from different teachers or algorithms without explicit mode labels, simplifying data curation for post-training. It also reframes the challenge of heterogeneous reasoning from global mode selection to local error correction, which could inform more targeted fine-tuning and inference-time intervention strategies. The study uses a verifiable symbolic graph-search environment with paired breadth-first and depth-first search trajectories, and the environment is open-sourced at https://github.com/Bowen1911/Symbolic_graph_env. The findings hold across model sizes from 0.6B to 14B and data budgets, but the analysis is limited to this controlled setting and may not fully generalize to natural language reasoning tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) is a common post-training method where a model learns from input-output pairs, often used to instill reasoning abilities. In practice, SFT datasets may mix reasoning traces from different teachers or algorithms, such as breadth-first search (BFS) and depth-first search (DFS), which represent distinct strategies. This paper asks whether a single model can learn such heterogeneous strategies without being told which mode to use, and where the remaining errors come from.

## [GaokaoMathGen: Generating Gaokao Math Finale Problems via Latent Reasoning Structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31823) ⭐️ 6.0/10

GaokaoMathGen, a new framework, extracts standardized latent reasoning structures from seed problems and uses them as structural controls (skills, methods, core identity, pivot step) to generate Chinese Gaokao mathematics finale problems. It also estimates realized difficulty through logical-quality filtering, fixed-solver decoding effort, structural features, and solver-output search signals, with 78.0% of 609 scored candidates passing the logical-quality filter and all 60 highest-effort candidates judged by teachers to reach finale or penultimate difficulty. This work moves beyond surface-level specifications for LLM-based problem generation by controlling the underlying mathematical kernel and solution path, which could enable more reliable creation of high-difficulty educational content. It also introduces a multi-signal approach to estimate realized difficulty, addressing a key limitation of prompted targets in educational assessment. The framework uses semantic normalization to improve cross-annotator comparability, and difficulty estimation combines logical-quality filtering, fixed-solver decoding effort, structural features, and solver-output search signals. In a teacher audit of the 60 highest-effort candidates, all were judged to reach finale or penultimate problem difficulty, with only one exhibiting a severe logical issue.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Chinese Gaokao mathematics finale problems are high-stakes exam items that require not only specific skills but also a deep mathematical kernel and solution path, making them hard to generate automatically. Latent reasoning structures refer to compact, abstract representations of reasoning processes extracted from seed problems, as opposed to surface-level textual specifications. Fixed-solver decoding effort measures how much computation a fixed solver needs to solve a problem, serving as a proxy for difficulty.

## [Early Exit for Generative Verifiers Cuts Resident Service by 85%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32931) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Prefill1, a method that stops a frozen 2B span verifier right after its decision token, using 84.84% less eager resident service than full generation on 2,015 code-agent test responses while maintaining F1 scores (83.43 vs. 83.41). This optimization significantly reduces inference costs for generative verifiers used in code agents and other LLM applications, making real-time verification more practical without sacrificing accuracy. Prefill1 supplies seven fixed syntax tokens and generates only the unrestricted branch token, saving 31.98% over Native8 despite identical logical token totals; under default compiled execution, savings remain 6.74-26.73% with small decision changes.

Early exit techniques aim to reduce inference cost by stopping generation once a decision is made, but they must balance efficiency with accuracy. This work applies early exit to a span verifier that flags unsupported claims in code-agent reports.

## [FIGR: Fine-Grained Instruction-Guided Graph Reasoning for VLN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11006) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces FIGR, a framework that enhances vision-and-language navigation by disentangling angular and visual cues in a graph and using adaptive open vocabulary guidance for fine-grained instruction understanding. On validation-unseen splits, FIGR achieves 67 SPL on R2R and 64.8 sDTW on RxR, surpassing SPENav by 1 percentage point in SPL and PRET by 2.4 points in sDTW. This work advances embodied AI by improving an agent's ability to follow complex, multilingual navigation instructions, which is crucial for real-world applications like service robots and autonomous vehicles. The open-vocabulary approach allows generalization to unseen entities and expressions, potentially making navigation systems more robust and adaptable. FIGR introduces an observation graph interaction mechanism to disentangle angular and visual cues with geometric embedding, an Adaptive Open Vocabulary Guidance (AOVG) module with a contextual role parser to avoid exact string matching, and a Multilingual Semantic Adapter (MSA) to map language-specific representations into a shared space. However, it is a specialized arXiv preprint without accompanying code or community discussion, limiting immediate impact.

## [DRAFT: LLM method for generating architectural design decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.08207) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes DRAFT (Domain-specific Retrieval Augmented Few-shot Tuning), a two-phase approach that fine-tunes LLMs on retrieval-augmented prompts from historical Architectural Decision Records (ADRs) and then generates new decisions or complete ADRs by combining retrieved examples with the fine-tuned model. Evaluated on 4,344 ADRs across multiple LLMs, DRAFT consistently produced outputs better aligned with real-world ADRs according to standard NLP metrics. Architectural Decision Records are important for capturing design rationale but are often skipped due to manual effort; DRAFT shows how LLMs can automate this, reducing dependence on proprietary models and addressing privacy, resource, and cost concerns for organizations. This could encourage broader adoption of ADRs in software development. While automatic metrics favored DRAFT, a human evaluation with the authors and external architects found no uniformly preferred approach, indicating that LLM-generated ADRs may still require human judgment. The method operates in an offline fine-tuning phase and an online generation phase, and it maintains practical efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

DRAFT combines retrieval-augmented few-shot prompting—where relevant examples are retrieved to guide the model—with fine-tuning to improve output quality.

## [Prompt Engineer Roles Rare, Skill Profile Distinct, LinkedIn Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00058) ⭐️ 6.0/10

An analysis of 20,662 LinkedIn job postings from April 2025 found that prompt engineer roles made up less than 0.5% of postings across five data and AI roles, with only 72 dedicated prompt engineer postings. The study also identified a distinct skill profile for these roles, emphasizing AI knowledge (22.8%), communication (21.9%), prompt design (18.7%), and creative problem-solving (15.8%). This empirical evidence counters speculation that prompt engineer is a burgeoning profession, suggesting instead that prompt engineering is a valuable skill across many roles rather than a standalone job title. The findings can help job seekers, employers, and educators better position prompt engineering in the evolving AI workforce. The snapshot predates widespread adoption of LLM-based agents, so the data may capture prompt engineering under favorable conditions; the authors note that whether the share has since declined is an open question. The study is a preprint (arXiv:2506.00058v3) and has not undergone peer review.

SACA introduces a density-based clustering algorithm inspired by selective attention that minimizes parameter tuning by adaptively filtering sparse points and outliers before refining clusters. ## [Fixed Points and Envelope Dynamics on Credal Hyperspaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04769) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper establishes that every inclusion-monotone, Hausdorff-continuous self-map on a credal order interval has least and greatest fixed points, obtained as limits of iteration from the interval endpoints. It also constructs lower and upper envelope maps for time-varying updates, and shows that under uniform W1 contraction of Markov semigroups, the credal evolution has a unique invariant credal set with exponential convergence. This provides rigorous fixed-point foundations for update rules on credal sets, which are central to imprecise probabilistic machine learning for robustness and safety. The envelope and convergence results offer theoretical guarantees for analyzing stability of uncertainty representations over time. The results rely on credal order intervals being complete lattices under the Hausdorff metric induced by weak convergence, and the convergence is uniform over all switching sequences and initial credal sets. Perturbation bounds are derived from finite-time Nisio discrepancies and one-step kernel set discrepancies, and an explicit reconstruction formula characterizes credal sets via countable support coordinates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Credal sets are convex sets of probability measures used in imprecise probability to represent uncertainty about which distribution governs a phenomenon. The hyperspace of such sets can be equipped with a Hausdorff metric based on weak convergence, and fixed-point theorems like Knaster-Tarski guarantee fixed points for monotone maps on complete lattices. This paper applies these ideas to study how credal sets evolve under repeated or time-varying updates.

## [Game-Theoretic Model Explains Rational Stagnation in Cooperative Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22232) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces a game-theoretic model of 'rational stagnation' where a rational adversary with potential-loss utility sustains suboptimal equilibria, and analyzes strategic regimes using a Bellman-style approach. Starting from the Prisoner's Dilemma, it shows that a transformation of utilities generates a fragile cooperation band where both cooperation and defection are equilibria. This work offers a novel framework for understanding why cooperative systems can remain stuck in suboptimal states, with potential applications to social-media algorithms and political trust. It highlights how adversarial rationality can deliberately preserve fragility, which could inform the design of more robust systems. The model extends to a dynamic setting with stochastic cooperative payoffs and intervention costs, yielding three strategic regimes: immediate destruction, rational stagnation, and intervention abandonment. The appendix generalizes the utility to a reference-dependent nonlinear form and proves its stability under reference shifts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Game theory studies strategic interactions where each player's outcome depends on the choices of others. The Prisoner's Dilemma is a classic example where rational players may end up in a suboptimal equilibrium. Bellman-style analysis refers to dynamic programming techniques that break down optimization problems into simpler subproblems, often used in dynamic games.

## [LoDADA: Localized Dynamics-Aware Domain Adaptation for Off-Dynamics Offline RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.21072) ⭐️ 6.0/10

LoDADA introduces a localized dynamics-aware domain adaptation method that clusters source and target transitions and uses domain discrimination to estimate cluster-level dynamics discrepancy, selectively retaining source transitions from clusters with small mismatch. It claims to outperform state-of-the-art off-dynamics offline RL methods across environments with diverse global and local dynamics shifts. This work addresses a key challenge in offline RL: how to safely reuse abundant source data when the target environment's dynamics differ, which is common in robotics and healthcare where target data is scarce. The cluster-based selection offers a scalable alternative to expensive per-sample filtering, potentially making off-dynamics offline RL more practical for real-world deployment. LoDADA avoids both overly coarse global assumptions and costly pointwise filtering by operating at the cluster level, and the paper provides theoretical insights alongside extensive experiments. However, the approach depends on the quality of clustering and domain discrimination, and its performance may vary with the number and nature of local dynamics shifts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Offline reinforcement learning aims to learn policies from a fixed dataset without online interaction. In off-dynamics offline RL, the agent has limited target-domain data but abundant source-domain data collected under different transition dynamics, and the goal is to leverage source data to improve target performance. Domain adaptation techniques, such as domain discrimination, are used to measure and mitigate the mismatch between source and target distributions.
