## [AESP: A Human-Sovereign Economic Protocol for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00318) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AESP is a layered protocol that enables AI agents to transact autonomously on crypto-native infrastructure while remaining cryptographically bound to human-defined governance boundaries. It enforces this through a deterministic eight-check policy engine, human-in-the-loop review tiers, EIP-712 dual-signed commitments with escrow, HKDF-based context-isolated privacy, and an ACE-GF-based cryptographic substrate, and is implemented as an open-source TypeScript SDK with 208 tests and interoperability via MCP and A2A. As AI agents increasingly perform economic tasks on behalf of humans, this protocol addresses the fundamental tension between agent autonomy and human control over financial assets, potentially enabling safer deployment of autonomous agents in financial contexts. It could influence standards for AI agent economic governance and privacy-preserving settlement in the blockchain ecosystem. The protocol formalizes two testable hypotheses on security coverage and latency overhead, and specifies a complete evaluation methodology with baselines and ablation design, but no empirical results are reported yet. The TypeScript SDK includes ten modules and 208 tests, and the protocol is designed for interoperability via MCP and A2A.

**Background**: AI agents are increasingly used to perform economic tasks such as trading and payments, raising concerns about how much autonomy they should have over financial assets. EIP-712 is a standard for signing typed structured data on Ethereum, enabling secure off-chain authorization. HKDF is a key derivation function used to derive multiple keys from a shared secret, often for isolating contexts. ACE-GF refers to a generative framework for atomic cryptographic entities, providing a cryptographic substrate for the protocol.

## [First-Class Relationships as a Representation Threshold for Operational Records](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13603) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2603.13603v2) argues that storing operational records with n-ary, temporal, confidence-bearing, and causal relationships requires first-class relationships—identified, attribute-carrying, and composable at every arity—and shows these suffice for all four requirements. The paper introduces the Attributed Temporal Causal Hypergraph (ATCH) model that meets all four requirements with query operators for each. This work provides a formal framework for representing complex operational records directly, which could influence the design of data models and query languages for domains like access control, incident analysis, and provenance tracking. It offers a clear criterion (the threshold) to evaluate and compare existing models such as SQL, property graphs, RDF 1.2, and TypeDB. The paper defines four storage requirements—n-ary relationships with attributes, temporal validity, confidence with an evidential basis, and causal links—and shows that each requires relationships to have identity and, for the latter two, composition. It demonstrates that first-class relationships are a threshold belonging to the relationship construct itself, not the surrounding data model, and illustrates this with a SQL implementation that builds a first-class construct from other parts.

## [Manifold Matching Regularization Aligns Autoencoder Latent Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16568) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Manifold Matching, a regularization method that minimizes the batch-wise mean squared error between pairwise distances in a reference representation R and those in an autoencoder's latent space Z. This label-free approach, called the Manifold-Matching Autoencoder (MMAE), allows any reference representation to guide the geometry of any bottleneck, and across fifteen datasets it statistically ties RTD-AE on distance correlation and triplet accuracy while remaining competitive on local and topological metrics. This work offers a simple, computationally cheap way to impose explicit geometric structure on latent spaces without labels, which could make representation learning more controllable and interpretable. Because the reference can be any representation, the method turns fixed embeddings into reusable out-of-sample encoders, potentially benefiting visualization, clustering, and downstream tasks that rely on meaningful latent geometry. The objective only requires scalar pairwise distances from the reference, so the dimensionality of R is decoupled from that of Z, and the computational cost is that of a plain autoencoder with a batched distance loss, avoiding persistence computation. All experiments use a two-dimensional bottleneck, and the authors frame MMAE not as a universal winner but as an efficient and controllable regularizer whose reference selects the inherited structure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Autoencoders are neural networks that compress data into a low-dimensional latent space (the bottleneck) and reconstruct the input from it. The geometry of this latent space—how distances and neighborhoods are arranged—strongly affects downstream tasks, so researchers often add regularizers to shape it. Manifold learning is the study of low-dimensional structures in high-dimensional data, and pairwise distances are a basic way to characterize such structures. Manifold Matching builds on this by using distances from a reference representation as a target for the latent space.

Researchers propose a hierarchical two-stage framework that combines a coarse long-term predictor with a grid-aware short-term predictor using a Spatio-Temporal Graph Transformer and environmental cross-modal attention. On Australian Craft Tracking System data, it outperforms the state-of-the-art by 25% in Average Displacement Error and 17% in Final Displacement Error for a 10-hour prediction horizon. Evaluation uses 3-hour input and 10-hour prediction horizon on North West Australian CTS data aligned with Copernicus Marine Service products, with ablation studies validating each component.

## [HCLM Framework Controls Learning via Observable Entropy and Risk Limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22940) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Human-Centered Learning Mechanics (HCLM), a framework that treats learning as a controlled process governed by task progress, representation information, structural restrictions, and externally specified risk limits. It validates the framework with a 288-run neural study and follow-up experiments, including a nuisance control study on Fashion-MNIST that improves shifted accuracy over ERM. This work offers a theoretical foundation for controlling learning dynamics through observable quantities and risk constraints, which could lead to more reliable and certifiable machine learning systems. It is particularly relevant for the ML theory community interested in bridging information theory, control theory, and empirical risk management. The framework characterizes learning via a normalized covariance-information force, metric observability, and exact feasible rate constraints, showing that the projected task rate depends on an observable cross-product that differs from the ordinary task information product when the probe set does not contain the training task. The study separates information control from empirical risk protection and finds cases where extra compression worsens held-out loss, while the nuisance study shows that generic protected balancing accounts for most of the gain over ERM.

**Background**: Human-Centered Learning Mechanics (HCLM) is a theoretical framework that models learning as a controlled dynamical process, incorporating concepts from control theory and information theory. The framework aims to provide certifiable guarantees for population risk by separating information control from empirical risk protection.

## [AiFlow proposes executable conformance contract for versioned LLM services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00558) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AiFlow introduces an executable construction-to-retirement conformance contract that links request admission, invocation, and version retirement for versioned LLM services. Each admitted request captures an action bound to checked operators, and the service certifies the call's terminal outcome and release of registered work before the owner retires a version. This addresses a real systems problem: live updates can bypass filters and concurrency limits, and background tasks may use stale versions after callers return. The contract mechanism could improve safety and correctness in production LLM serving systems that need to update models or workflows without downtime. The evaluation covered two stateless linear workflows in a single Java virtual machine; with both construction and completion checks, all nine injected fault observations were contained, versus six of nine with either check alone. Deferred publication met a 500 ms deadline more often at 20 requests/s, while strict publication led at 80 requests/s.

## [Online Non-Monotone DR-Submodular Maximization Achieves 0.4013 Approximation with Feasible Queries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02145) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper presents an online algorithm for non-monotone DR-submodular maximization over convex down-closed sets that achieves a 0.4013 approximation coefficient using only feasible gradient queries, improving on the previous 1/e bound. The algorithm plays a random action, queries one gradient at a feasible point, and makes one projection per round, with expected approximate regret O(RB(1+R)√T). This improves the best-known approximation guarantee for online DR-submodular maximization when gradients can only be queried at feasible points, a setting that arises in real-world applications where infeasible queries are not allowed. It advances the theoretical foundations of online submodular optimization, which has applications in machine learning, resource allocation, and combinatorial optimization. The algorithm maintains a state in K and uses a coordinate-chain comparison inside the box [0,x] to keep all queries feasible, with a second comparison along the direction x to cancel extra terms. Because queries are feasible, the algorithm also yields regret O(T^{2/3}) under semi-bandit feedback and O(T^{4/5}) under bandit feedback with bounded values, and it retains the √T rate for unbiased stochastic gradients with bounded second moment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Submodular functions capture diminishing returns and are widely used to model combinatorial problems such as influence maximization and sensor placement. DR-submodularity extends this notion to continuous domains, and online settings involve adversarial sequences of functions where the algorithm must choose actions without knowing future functions. Previous online algorithms achieving coefficients like 4/9 or 2√3−3 relied on gradient queries along chains that leave the feasible set, which is impractical when queries must be feasible.

## [TryOnReward: Foveated Reward Model for Virtual Try-On Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13259) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TryOnReward introduces a fine-grained, foveation-calibrated vision-language reward model for reinforcement fine-tuning of virtual try-on (VTON), designed to reduce reward hacking caused by weak fidelity metrics. The authors also release TryOnRank-100K, a human-annotated per-dimension rating dataset, plus TryOn-Bench and TryOnRewardBench benchmarks, and show that their reward model outperforms generic judges in human preference alignment. Virtual try-on quality hinges on preserving fine garment and person details, yet classic fidelity metrics correlate poorly with human judgment, so using them as RFT rewards leads to severe reward hacking. A dedicated, preference-aligned reward model could make reinforcement fine-tuning of VTON generators more reliable and produce results humans actually prefer, affecting both e-commerce try-on systems and broader visual generation reward modeling. TryOnReward builds on a vision-language backbone and uses a foveation calibration objective that grounds each quality dimension in its relevant image region to avoid global shortcut learning, while jointly optimizing pairwise preferences and per-dimension quality scores via margin-aware supervision. The approach leverages both relative and absolute quality signals, and experiments across multiple baselines show it consistently yields human-preferred try-on results when used as the RFT reward function.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Virtual try-on (VTON) aims to dress a person in a reference garment while producing visually plausible results, and reinforcement fine-tuning (RFT) is increasingly used to steer generative models toward human-preferred outputs. RFT depends on a reward function that scores outputs; when that scorer is a weak proxy such as classic fidelity metrics, the model learns to exploit the metric rather than genuinely improve, a failure mode known as reward hacking. Vision-language models (VLMs) are commonly used as reward backbones, but generic VLMs lack the fine-grained discriminative ability needed to judge garment and person detail preservation.

<a id="item-396"></a>
## [Geometric View of Reward Updates in Inverse Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14041) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces an occupancy-space geometric perspective on reward updates in inverse reinforcement learning, showing that the geometry along the e-geodesic toward the optimal occupancy affects the dual gradient. It proposes preconditioning the dual gradient with the average Fisher information along the occupancy trajectory, yielding efficient natural-gradient-style updates. This work bridges information geometry and IRL, offering a principled way to accelerate and stabilize reward learning. It could influence the design of more sample-efficient IRL algorithms and provide theoretical convergence guarantees. The preconditioned gradient is shown to be a convex interpolation of the old reward and a new target reward, enabling natural-gradient updates with little computational overhead. The authors also analyze the updates from a mirror descent perspective and provide convergence results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Inverse reinforcement learning (IRL) aims to infer a reward function from observed expert behavior, typically formulated as a bilevel optimization problem solved via Lagrangian methods. Occupancy space represents the distribution of state-action visits, and information geometry studies the manifold of probability distributions using concepts like the Fisher information matrix and e-geodesics. Natural gradient descent uses the Fisher information to precondition gradients, improving optimization efficiency.

<a id="item-397"></a>
## [AffectWorld: New Benchmark Tests Emotion-Driven World Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18305) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced AffectWorld, a benchmark that evaluates how well models predict future world states by chaining emotion, action, and world-state consistency. It comprises 1,498 annotated everyday video samples and a counterfactual subset of 223 samples, with seven metrics plus a counterfactual metric, and evaluations of eight state-of-the-art world models (e.g., Cosmos 3) revealed low scores on world-state change (0.064–0.240) and counterfactual emotion sensitivity (0.027–0.070) on a [0,1] scale. Existing world model benchmarks mainly assess physical plausibility and perceptual quality, leaving emotion-driven world evolution largely untested. AffectWorld fills this gap by providing a testbed for developing world models that capture how emotions shape future physical and social states, which could impact research in video understanding, social reasoning, and human-centered AI. The benchmark defines three tasks—World-State Consistency, Emotion-Action Consistency, and Causal Consistency—and uses a combination of automatic visual assessment and human-calibrated VLM/LLM judgments. The counterfactual subset tests whether changing an agent's emotion under fixed scene and event conditions yields corresponding changes in actions and consequences, revealing substantial gaps in generating consequences of emotion-driven actions and adapting predictions to emotional interventions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: World models are AI systems that learn to simulate and predict the future evolution of an environment, and they are increasingly evaluated on benchmarks that measure physical plausibility and perceptual quality. However, human behavior is also shaped by emotions, and predicting how emotions influence actions and subsequent world states is a crucial but overlooked aspect. AffectWorld builds on concepts from causal reasoning and counterfactual thinking to assess whether models can understand the chain from emotion to action to world state.

<a id="item-398"></a>
## [Set Transformer predicts X-ray transmission in polymer composites in milliseconds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28709) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers present STAR-XPC, a Set Transformer neural network that maps the unordered set of molecular constituents, their mass fractions, and four bulk descriptors of a polymer composite directly to the X-ray transmission ratio I(t)/I0 in 1–3 milliseconds. Trained on 4,697 PENELOPE/penEasy Monte Carlo simulations, the model achieves R² = 0.9988 and a median absolute percentage error of 1.15%, compared with over 5 hours per reference simulation. This work shows that a permutation-invariant neural surrogate can replace hours-long Monte Carlo transport simulations with near-instant inference at high accuracy, which could dramatically accelerate the design and screening of radiation-shielding polymer composites. It also demonstrates a practical template for combining chemistry-pretrained language model embeddings with set-based architectures in materials science. The model is permutation invariant by construction, accepts mixtures of up to 11 constituents in a fixed-size padded set, and embeds each constituent with the chemistry-pretrained ChemBERTa language model. Evaluation used leakage-free, jointly stratified 10-fold cross-validation with MSE = (1.12 ± 0.56)×10⁻⁴ and an alpha-trimmed (α = 0.10) absolute percentage error of 1.66%, though the dataset is limited to polyethylene-based slabs 0.15–2.5 mm thick at 30, 80, 100, and 120 kV tube voltages.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Monte Carlo transport simulation, such as the PENELOPE/penEasy code, tracks individual photon histories to estimate how much X-ray radiation passes through a material, and is highly accurate but computationally expensive because billions of histories are needed for stable results. A Set Transformer is a neural architecture designed to process unordered sets of elements while remaining invariant to their ordering, making it well suited to representing material compositions whose constituents have no inherent sequence. ChemBERTa is a transformer language model pretrained on chemical structures, used here to turn each molecular constituent into a meaningful embedding vector.

<a id="item-399"></a>
## [ReliaGuard: Reconstruction-Aware Watermarking for Edit Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16368) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ReliaGuard introduces a reconstruction-aware watermarking framework that distinguishes weak watermark responses caused by background reconstruction from those caused by intended edits, enabling reliable source attribution and blind edit localization. It achieves intersection-over-union scores of 85.61% for complete editing outputs and 94.90% for locally spliced outputs at a 1% false-positive rate, exceeding the strongest baseline by 45.02 percentage points, with 100% identifier-bit accuracy. This work addresses a critical ambiguity in blind localization for generative image editing, where background reconstruction can mask watermark evidence, and provides a robust solution for source attribution and edit detection. It could impact digital forensics, copyright protection, and content authenticity verification in an era of increasingly sophisticated generative editing tools. The framework uses reconstruction-stable evidence writing that aligns keyed feature responses in the protected image and its VAE reconstruction, a quantization-aware projection to bound distortion in the eight-bit image, and a coarse-to-fine blind reader that combines keyed responses with image context. It requires only the received image and key at inference and remains effective across seven image-processing families, with localization scores of 81.01% and 86.49% and identifier-bit accuracies around 92.8%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Generative image editing models often use variational autoencoders (VAEs) to encode and reconstruct images, which can alter background regions outside the intended edit. Watermarking embeds hidden signals for source attribution, but reconstruction can weaken these signals, creating ambiguity in blind localization—determining where edits occurred without the original image. Quantization-aware training simulates low-precision effects to maintain performance after compression to eight-bit images.

<a id="item-400"></a>
## [AltRA-Test: A Bootstrap Statistical Test for LLM Judges Without Shared Annotators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26073) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose AltRA-Test, a bootstrap-based statistical test that determines when an LLM can replace a human annotator even when items are annotated by different subsets of annotators, without requiring shared annotators across items. The test resamples items, draws k human annotations per item, and substitutes one with an LLM annotation to compare inter-annotator agreement (IAA) between all-human and mixed human-LLM panels, and is shown to be a consistent test of asymptotic level alpha. As LLMs are increasingly used as judges and graders to replace human annotators, this test addresses a practical gap in large-scale annotation workflows where annotators are not shared across items, providing a statistically justified basis for replacement decisions. It could affect researchers and practitioners designing annotation pipelines and evaluating LLM-as-judge systems. The test uses a bootstrap that resamples items and creates paired all-human and mixed human-LLM panels, supporting replacement when the mixed panel's IAA is statistically non-inferior to the all-human panel's IAA. Evaluations across 18 LLMs, 5 prompting techniques, and 20 datasets spanning text, image, audio, and video show that about 100 items often suffice, and LLMs pass the test in roughly half of real-world setups depending mainly on the task.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) measures how consistently different human annotators label the same items, and it is a key signal of annotation quality. Bootstrapping is a resampling technique that estimates the distribution of a statistic by repeatedly sampling from data with replacement, which allows statistical tests without strong distributional assumptions. The original Alternative Annotator Test (Alt-Test) assumed a dense setting where all items are annotated by the same annotators, which AltRA-Test relaxes.

<a id="item-401"></a>
## [SN-EDA: Skew-Normal Estimation of Distribution Algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26938) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SN-EDA replaces Gaussian sampling in estimation of distribution algorithms with a skew-normal distribution whose skewness follows the cumulative mean-shift path, improving convergence on sloped landscapes and significantly outperforming Gaussian EDA in five of six benchmark settings. This work offers a simple, low-overhead modification to standard Gaussian EDAs that mitigates premature convergence on slopes, potentially benefiting practitioners using EDAs for continuous optimization without requiring changes to the estimation step. The skewness is applied through a gate that the pre-registered test finds dispensable, and while SN-EDA improves over Gaussian EDA, it remains behind algorithms that also correct the second moment; on a linear function, it raises the fraction of variance surviving selection from 0.29 to 0.53.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Estimation of distribution algorithms (EDAs) are evolutionary algorithms that build and sample explicit probabilistic models of promising solutions, often using a multivariate Gaussian. Truncation selection, which keeps only the fittest individuals, can remove most of the variance along the gradient on sloped landscapes, causing premature convergence. The skew-normal distribution generalizes the normal distribution to allow for non-zero skewness, and this paper uses it to correct the third moment instead of enlarging variance or shifting the mean.

<a id="item-402"></a>
## [RASA-Bench evaluates source-grounded Hindi prose refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29750) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RASA-Bench introduces a four-layer protocol to evaluate how well models refine under-written Hindi prose drafts while staying faithful to the source, testing eight refiners on 2,050 drafts (16,400 rewrites) across three prose forms. The benchmark reveals form-dependent faithfulness failure rates ranging from 9.1% on short stories to 29.5% on audio fiction, and shows that the type of drift that disqualifies a rewrite shifts from events and identity in narrative to claims and evidence in essays. This benchmark addresses a gap in multilingual NLP evaluation by focusing on the editing task—refining under-written AI drafts—rather than writing from scratch, which is crucial for creative prose production. Its findings on faithfulness drift and form-sensitive preference highlight the need for source-grounded evaluation in low-resource languages like Hindi, impacting developers of multilingual text-editing tools. The four-layer protocol credits a rewrite only after it passes a faithfulness check against its own source, then ranks survivors by holistic editorial preference using per-form Bradley-Terry fits with standardized centered coefficients. A 600-case human audit shows gate agreement of 0.925 (0.91–0.94 across forms), while subjective preference is least settled for essays among both human readers and the model judge.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 27, 05:00

**Background**: Translationese refers to systematic linguistic patterns that arise in translated text due to source-language interference, often making it sound unnatural. Source-grounded text refinement requires models to improve a draft while preserving factual consistency with the original source, a task evaluated by faithfulness metrics that check whether claims are directly supported by the source. RASA-Bench extends these concepts to Hindi creative prose, where under-written drafts may contain translationese and subtle alterations of source content.
